Since there are  $\Omega(H/2)$  bandit problems, plugging in the choice of  $\delta$ , we find that the total regret is  $\Omega(dH\sqrt{KH})$  and the result follows by noting that  $T = KH$ .

Remark 5.7. Theorem 5.6 shows that for any algorithm running on episodic linear mixture MDPs, its regret is lower bounded by  $\Omega(dH\sqrt{T})$ . The lower bound together with the upper bound of UCRL-VTR $^+$  in Theorem 5.3 shows that UCRL-VTR $^+$  is minimax optimal up to logarithmic factors.

Remark 5.8. Our lower bound analysis can be adapted to prove a lower bound for linear MDPs proposed in (Yang and Wang, 2019a; Jin et al., 2020). In specific, based on our constructed linear mixture MDP  $M$  in the proof sketch of Theorem 5.6, we can construct a linear MDP  $\bar{M}(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},H,\{\bar{r}_h\},\{\bar{\mathbb{P}}_h\})$  as follows. For each stage  $h\in [H]$ , the transition probability kernel  $\bar{\mathbb{P}}_h$  and the reward function  $\bar{r}_h$  are defined as  $\bar{\mathbb{P}}_h(s'|s,\mathbf{a}) = \langle \phi (s,\mathbf{a}),\pmb {\mu}_h(s')\rangle$  and  $\bar{r}_h(s,\mathbf{a}) = \langle \phi (s,\mathbf{a}),\pmb {\xi}_h\rangle$ , where  $\phi (s,a),\pmb {\mu}(s^{\prime})\in \mathbb{R}^{d + 1}$  are two feature mappings, and  $\pmb {\xi}_h\in \mathbb{R}^{d + 1}$  is a parameter vector. Here, we choose  $\phi (s,\mathbf{a}),\pmb {\mu}_h(s'),\pmb {\xi}_h\in \mathbb{R}^{d + 1}$  as follows:

$$
\phi (s, \mathbf {a}) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} (\alpha , \beta \mathbf {a} ^ {\top}, 0) ^ {\top}, & s = x _ {h}, h \in [ H + 1 ]; \\ (0, \mathbf {0} ^ {\top}, 1) ^ {\top}, & s = x _ {H + 2}. \end{array} \right., \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h} (s ^ {\prime}) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} ((1 - \delta) / \alpha , - \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h} ^ {\top} / \beta , 0) ^ {\top}, & s ^ {\prime} = x _ {h + 1}; \\ (\delta / \alpha , \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h} ^ {\top} / \beta , 1) ^ {\top}, & s ^ {\prime} = x _ {H + 2}; \\ \mathbf {0}, & \mathrm {o t h e r w i s e}, \end{array} \right.
$$

and  $\pmb{\xi}_h = (\mathbf{0}^\top, 1)^\top$ . It can be verified that  $\max \{\|\pmb{\xi}_h\|_2, \|\pmb{\mu}_h(\mathcal{S})\|_2\} \leq \sqrt{d + 1}$ , and  $\|\phi(s, \mathbf{a})\|_2 \leq 1$  for any  $(s, a) \in \mathcal{S} \times \mathcal{A}$ . In addition, for any  $h \in [H]$ , we have  $\mathbb{P}_h(s'|s, a) = \bar{\mathbb{P}}_h(s'|s, a)$  and  $r_h(s, a) = \bar{r}_h(s, a)$  when  $s = x_h$  or  $x_{H+2}$ . Since at stage  $h$ ,  $s$  can be either  $x_h$  or  $x_{H+2}$ , we can show that the constructed linear MDP  $\bar{M}$  has the same transition probability as the linear mixture MDP  $M$ , which suggests the same lower bound  $\Omega(dH\sqrt{T})$  in Theorem 5.6 also holds for linear MDP.

# 6 Optimal Exploration for Discounted MDPs

In this section, we consider infinite-horizon discounted linear mixture MDPs. We propose the UCLK $^{+}$  algorithm, which is a counterpart of UCRL-VTR $^{+}$  for the discounted MDP setting. We also provide a regret analysis, which shows that the algorithm, apart from logarithmic factors, is nearly minimax optimal.

# 6.1 Proposed Algorithm

The details of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  are described in Algorithm 3. Recall that  $\bar{H} = 1 / (1 - \gamma)$  is the effective horizon length of the discounted MDP.  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  is built upon the previous algorithm UCLK proposed by Zhou et al. (2020), with a modified bonus term. Just like UCLK,  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  is a multi-epoch algorithm inspired by Jaksch et al. (2010); Abbasi-Yadkori et al. (2011); Lattimore and Hutter (2012). The  $k$ -th epoch of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  starts at round  $t_k$  and ends at round  $t_{k + 1} - 1$ . The length of each epoch is not prefixed but depends on previous observations. In each epoch,  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  maintains a set of plausible MDPs through confidence set  $\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_t$  which includes the underlying  $\pmb{\theta}^*$  with high probability, then computes the action-value function corresponding to the near-optimal MDP among all the plausible MDPs by calling the Extended Value Iteration (EVI) sub-procedure in Algorithm 4. At a high level, EVI provides the optimistic estimate of  $Q^{*}(\cdot ,\cdot)$  within a given confidence set  $\mathcal{C}$ , similar to the optimistic value function estimator in Line 4 of Algorithm 2. More details about EVI can be found in Lattimore and Hutter (2012); Dann and Brunskill (2015); Zhou et al. (2020). The multi-epoch structure and the use of EVI are the main differences between  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  and its episodic counterpart UCRL-VTR $^+$  proposed in Section 5.

The key difference between  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  and UCLK lies in the regression-based estimator. In particular,  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  adapts the weighted ridge regression proposed in Section 5 to calculate its

Algorithm 3 UCLK+ for Discounted Linear Mixture MDPs  
Require: Regularization parameter  $\lambda$ , an upper bound  $B$  of the  $\ell_2$ -norm of  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$ , number of value iteration rounds  $U$   
1:Receive  $s_1$ , set  $k\gets 0,t_0\gets 1$   
2:Set  $\widehat{\Sigma}_{1},\widetilde{\Sigma}_{1}\gets \lambda \mathbf{I},\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{1},\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{1},\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{1}\gets \mathbf{0},Q_{0}(\cdot ,\cdot),V_{0}(\cdot)\gets \bar{H},\pi_{0}(\cdot)\gets \operatorname{argmax}_{a\in \mathcal{A}}Q_{0}(\cdot ,a)$   
3:for  $t = 1,2,\ldots$  do  
4:if  $\operatorname*{det}(\widehat{\Sigma}_t)\leq 2\operatorname*{det}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t_k})$  then  
5:Set  $Q_{t}(\cdot ,\cdot)\gets Q_{t - 1}(\cdot ,\cdot),V_{t}(\cdot)\gets V_{t - 1}(\cdot),\pi_{t}(\cdot)\gets \pi_{t - 1}(\cdot)$  {Keep policy}  
6:else  
7:  $k\gets k + 1,t_k\gets t$  {New phase starts at  $t = t_k$ }  
8:Set  $\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_t$  as in (6.1)  $\{\widehat{C}_t$  is based on  $s_1,\dots ,s_t$  to be used by the policy in this round}  
9:Set  $Q_{t}(\cdot ,\cdot)\gets \mathrm{EVI}(\widehat{C}_{t},U),V_{t}(\cdot)\gets \max_{a\in \mathcal{A}}Q_{t}(\cdot ,a),\pi_{t}(\cdot)\gets \operatorname{argmax}_{a\in \mathcal{A}}Q_{t}(\cdot ,a)$   
10:end if  
11:Take action  $a_{t}\gets \pi_{t}(s_{t})$  receive  $s_{t + 1}\sim \mathbb{P}(\cdot |s_t,a_t)$   
12:Set  $[\bar{\nabla}_tV_t](s_t,a_t)$  as in (6.2),  $E_{t}$  as in (6.3)  
13:  $\bar{\sigma}_t\gets \sqrt{\max\left\{\bar{H}^2 / d,[\bar{\nabla}_tV_t](s_t,a_t) + E_t\right\}}$   
14: $\widehat{\Sigma}_{t + 1}\gets \widehat{\Sigma}_{t} + \bar{\sigma}_{t}^{-2}\phi_{V_{t}}(s_{t},a_{t})\phi_{V_{t}}(s_{t},a_{t})^{\top},\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{t + 1}\gets \widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{t} + \bar{\sigma}_{t}^{-2}\phi_{V_{t}}(s_{t},a_{t})V_{t}(s_{t + 1})$   
15: $\widetilde{\Sigma}_{t + 1}\gets \widetilde{\Sigma}_{t} + \phi_{V_{t}^{2}}(s_{t},a_{t})\phi_{V_{t}^{2}}(s_{t},a_{t}),\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{t + 1}\gets \widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{t} + \phi_{V_{t}^{2}}(s_{t},a_{t})V_{t}^{2}(s_{t + 1})$   
16: $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{t + 1}\gets \widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{t + 1}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}_{t + 1},\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{t + 1}\gets \widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{t + 1}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{b}}_{t + 1}$   
17:end for

estimate  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_t$ , instead of the regular ridge regression used in Zhou et al. (2020). The weight  $\bar{\sigma}_t$  is calculated in Line 14, which is an upper bound of the variance  $[\mathbb{V}_t V_t](s_t, a_t)$ , as the sum of an empirical variance estimate  $[\bar{\mathbb{V}}_t V_t](s_t, a_t)$  and the offset term  $E_t$ . As discussed in Section 5, we expect this weighting to give rise to a more accurate estimate of  $\pmb{\theta}^*$ . The computational efficiency of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  also depends on the specific family of feature mapping  $\phi(\cdot|\cdot,\cdot)$ . Suppose  $\phi(\cdot|\cdot,\cdot)$  has the same structure  $\phi(s'|s, a) = \psi(s') \odot \mu(s, a)$  as we have discussed in Section 5.1, and we have access to a  $O(\mathrm{poly}(d))$ -time integration oracle  $\mathcal{O}$ . Moreover, suppose we have access to a maximization oracle  $\mathcal{O}'$  which can solve the constrained linear maximization problem  $\max_{\pmb{\theta} \in \mathcal{C} \cap \mathcal{B}} \langle \pmb{\theta}, \mathbf{a} \rangle$  within polynomial time in  $d$ . Then it can be verified that the total computation cost of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  is also  $O(\mathrm{poly}(d)|\mathcal{A}| \bar{H}T)$ . The argument is easiest to explain in the case when  $\mathcal{O}$  needs the evaluation of  $V$  to compute  $\sum_{s'} \psi(s') V(s')$  at a fixed set  $S' \subset S$  of poly(d) size; - the generic case is similar to this. EVI will then compute a sequence of vectors  $\mathbf{m}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{m}_U \in \mathbb{R}^d$ . In particular, for  $u \in [U]$ ,  $\mathbf{m}_u = \sum_{s' \in S'} \psi(s') V^{(u-1)}(s')$ . This implies that at any  $(s, a) \in S \times \mathcal{A}$ ,  $Q^{(u)}(s, a)$  can be computed by evaluating  $r(s, a) + \gamma \max_{\pmb{\theta} \in \mathcal{C} \cap \mathcal{B}} \langle \pmb{\theta}, \mathbf{m}_{u-1} \odot \phi(s, a) \rangle$ , which can be done in polynomial time by our assumption on  $\mathcal{O}'$ . Then, to compute  $\mathbf{m}_u$ , one needs to compute  $\max_{a \in A} Q^{(u-1)}(s', a)$  at  $s' \in S'$ , which is again, polynomial, resulting in the claimed compute cost. We note in passing that this is an example of computation with a factored linear model (cf. Section 3, Pires and Szepesvári 2016).

# 6.2 Upper Bound

Now we provide the regret analysis for  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$ . First, we define confidence set  $\widehat{C}_t$ :

$$
\widehat {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} = \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta}: \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \leq \widehat {\beta} _ {t} \right\}, \tag {6.1}
$$

Algorithm 4 Extended Value Iteration:  $\operatorname{EVI}(\mathcal{C}, U)$

Require: Set  $\mathcal{C} \subset \mathbb{R}^d$ , number of value iteration rounds  $U$

1: Set  $Q^{(0)}(\cdot ,\cdot)\gets \bar{H}$  
2:  $Q(\cdot ,\cdot)\gets Q^{(0)}(\cdot ,\cdot)$  
3: if  $\mathcal{C} \cap \mathcal{B} \neq \emptyset$  then  
4: for  $u = 1,\ldots ,U$  do  
5: Let  $V^{(u - 1)}(\cdot) \gets \max_{a \in \mathcal{A}} Q^{(u - 1)}(\cdot, a)$  and

$$
Q ^ {(u)} (\cdot , \cdot) \leftarrow r (\cdot , \cdot) + \gamma \max  _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathcal {C} \cap \mathcal {B}} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \phi_ {V ^ {(u - 1)}} (\cdot , \cdot) \right\rangle .
$$

6: end for  
7: Let  $Q(\cdot, \cdot) \gets Q^{(U)}(\cdot, \cdot)$ .  
8: end if  
9: return  $Q(\cdot, \cdot)$

$$
\widehat {\beta} _ {t} = 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + t / \lambda) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B.
$$

Meanwhile, we define a set  $\mathcal{B}$  similar to that of Zhou et al. (2020). The set  $\mathcal{B}$  includes all possible  $\pmb{\theta}$  such that  $\langle \pmb{\theta},\phi (\cdot |s,a)\rangle$  is a probability distribution:

$$
\mathcal {B} = \{\boldsymbol {\theta} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d}: \langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \phi (\cdot | s, a) \rangle \text {i s a p r o b a b i l i t y d i s t r i b u t i o n f o r a l l} (s, a) \in \mathcal {S} \times \mathcal {A} \}.
$$

This set is non-empty as  $\theta^{*}\in \mathcal{B}$ . We also specify the empirical variance  $[\bar{\nabla}_tV_t](s_t,a_t)$  and offset term  $E_{t}$  as follows:

$$
[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) = \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}), \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, \bar {H} ^ {2} ]} - \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, \bar {H} ]} ^ {2}, \tag {6.2}
$$

$$
E _ {t} = \min  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2}, 2 \bar {H} \check {\beta} _ {t} \| \widehat {\mathbf {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \| _ {2} \right\} + \min  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2}, \widetilde {\beta} _ {t} \| \widetilde {\mathbf {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \| _ {2} \right\}. \tag {6.3}
$$

Here,  $\tilde{\beta}_t$  and  $\tilde{\beta}_t$  are defined by

$$
\check {\beta} _ {t} = 8 d \sqrt {\log (1 + t / \lambda) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B,
$$

$$
\widetilde {\beta} _ {t} = 8 \sqrt {d \bar {H} ^ {4} \log (1 + t \bar {H} ^ {4} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \bar {H} ^ {2} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B.
$$

The following key technical lemma guarantees that with high probability,  $\pmb{\theta}^{*}$  lies in a sequence of confidence sets. Meanwhile, the difference between the empirical variance  $[\bar{\mathbb{V}}_t V_t](s_t, a_t)$  and the true variance  $[\mathbb{V} V_t](s_t, a_t)$  can be bounded by the offset term  $E_t$ . Let  $\mathbb{P}$  be the probability distribution obtained over state-action sequences by using  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  in the MDP  $M$ . In the next two results, the probabilistic statements refer to this distribution.

Lemma 6.1. With probability at least  $1 - 3\delta$ , simultaneously for all  $1 \leq t \leq T$ , we have

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \in \widehat {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} \cap \mathcal {B}, | [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) | \leq E _ {t}.
$$

Proof. See Appendix D.1.

Remark 6.2. Currently our  $\widehat{\beta}_t, \check{\beta}_t, \widetilde{\beta}_t$  depend on a logarithmic term  $\log(4t^2/\delta)$ . This is due to a union bound over  $T$  events, each of which holds with probability  $\delta/(4t^2)$  for  $t \in [T]$ . This term can actually be tightened to  $\log(4d^2\log^2 t/\delta)$  by the fact that  $\widehat{C}_t$  only changes at the beginning of each epoch and there are at most  $d\log T$  epochs. Thus, by using a more dedicated empirical variance estimate  $[\bar{\mathbb{V}} V_t](\cdot, \cdot)$  based on a rarely-updated version of  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_t$  and  $\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_t$ , and applying a union bound on the  $d\log T$  events, we can sharpen the logarithmic term.

Equipped with Lemma 6.1, we are able to prove the following theorem about the regret of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$ .

Theorem 6.3. Let  $M_{\theta^*}$  be a discounted,  $B$ -bounded linear mixture MDP. Set  $\lambda = 1 / B^2$ . Then, with probability at least  $1 - 5\delta$ , the total regret of  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  on  $M_{\theta^*}$  is bounded by

$$
\mathrm {R e g r e t} \big (M _ {\pmb {\theta} ^ {*}}, T \big) = \widetilde {O} \Bigg (\sqrt {d ^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {3} + d \bar {H} ^ {4}} \sqrt {T} + d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d ^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {3} + \bar {H} T \gamma^ {U} \Bigg).
$$

Proof. See Appendix D.2.

Remark 6.4. Theorem 6.3 implies that when  $U = \lceil (1 - \gamma)^{-1} \log (T / (1 - \gamma)) \rceil$ ,  $d \geq (1 - \gamma)^{-1}$  and  $T \geq d^4 (1 - \gamma)^{-1} + d^3 (1 - \gamma)^{-2}$ , the regret is  $\widetilde{O}(d\sqrt{T} / (1 - \gamma)^{1.5})$ . Now note that Zhou et al. (2020) proved that for any algorithm, its regret on a discounted linear mixture MDP is lower bounded by  $\Omega(d\sqrt{T} / (1 - \gamma)^{1.5})$ . Hence, in the large  $d$  and large sample limit, the upper bound of the regret achieved by  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  matches the lower bound up to logarithmic factors. Therefore,  $\mathrm{UCLK}^{+}$  is nearly minimax optimal for discounted linear mixture MDPs in the said regime.

# 7 Conclusion and Future Work

In this paper, we considered RL with linear function approximation for linear mixture MDPs. We proposes a new Bernstein-type concentration inequality for self-normalized vector-valued martingales, which was shown to tighten existing confidence sets for linear bandits when the reward noise has low variance  $\sigma_t^2$  and is almost surely uniformly bounded by a constant  $R > 0$ . This also allowed us to derive a bandit algorithm for the stochastic linear bandit problem with changing actions sets. The proposed algorithm uses weighted least-squares estimates and achieves a second-order regret bound of order  $O(R\sqrt{dT} + d\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sigma_t^2})$ , which is a significant improvement on the dimension dependence in the low-noise regime. Based on the new tail inequality, we propose a new, computationally efficient algorithm, UCRL-VTR+ for episodic MDPs with an  $\widetilde{O}(dH\sqrt{T})$  regret, and the UCLK+ algorithm for discounted MDPs with an  $\widetilde{O}(d\sqrt{T}/(1-\gamma)^{1.5})$  regret. Both regret bounds match the corresponding lower bounds up to logarithmic factors, which shows that both algorithms are nearly minimax optimal.

We would like to point out that our current regret bounds are nearly minimax optimal only for the "large dimension" and "large sample" cases. In particular,  $\mathrm{UCRL - VTR^{+}}$  is nearly minimax optimal only when  $d\geq H$  and  $T\geq d^4 H^2 +d^3 H^3$  and  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  is nearly minimax optimal only when  $d\geq (1 - \gamma)^{-1}$  and  $T\geq d^4 (1 - \gamma)^{-1} + d^3 (1 - \gamma)^{-2}$ . It remains to be seen whether the range-restrictions on the dimension and the sample size can be loosened or altogether eliminated.

# A The Definition of State- and Action-value Functions for Nonstationary Policies in Discounted MDPs

The purpose of this section is to introduce a definition of the state and the action-value functions, which is consistent with the definition given in the introduction, but avoids the issue that the definition given there may be ill-defined. Fix a nonstationary policy  $\pi = (\pi_t)_{t\geq 1}$ , a state-action pair  $(s,a)\in S\times \mathcal{A}$ , a time index  $t\geq 1$  and a history  $h_t\coloneqq (s_1,a_1,s_2,a_2,\ldots ,a_{t - 1})\in (\mathcal{S}\times \mathcal{A})^{t - 1}$  of length  $t - 1$ . Let  $\mathbb{P}_{\pi ,h_t,s,a}$  be a probability distribution over  $(S\times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$ , where  $\mathbb{N} = \{1,2,\dots \}$  is the set of positive integers defined so that for a sequence  $(s_t,a_t,s_{t + 1},a_{t + 1},\ldots)\in (S\times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} _ {\pi , h _ {t}, s, a} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}, s _ {t + 1}, a _ {t + 1}, \dots\right) =
$$

$$
\mathbf {1} \left\{s _ {t} = s \right\} \mathbf {1} \left\{a _ {t} = a \right\} \times
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(s _ {t + 1} \mid s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \mathbf {1} \left\{a _ {t + 1} = \pi_ {t + 1} \left(s _ {t + 1}; s _ {1}, a _ {1}, \dots , s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\} \times
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(s _ {t + 2} \mid s _ {t + 1}, a _ {t + 1}\right) \mathbf {1} \left\{a _ {t + 2} = \pi_ {t + 2} \left(s _ {t + 2}; s _ {1}, a _ {1}, \dots , s _ {t + 1}, a _ {t + 1}\right) \right\} \times
$$

：

It is not hard to see that this is indeed a probability distribution. Denoting by  $\mathbb{E}_{\pi,h_t,s,a}$  the expectation corresponding to  $\mathbb{P}_{\pi,h_t,s,a}$ , the action value  $Q_{t}^{\pi}(s,a)$  is defined as

$$
Q _ {t} ^ {\pi} (s, a) = \mathbb {E} _ {\pi , h _ {t}, s, a} \left[ \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \gamma^ {i} r (s _ {t + i}, a _ {t + i}) \right].
$$

Note that  $Q_{t}^{\pi}$ , while the notation suppresses this dependence, is a function of  $h_t$ . The value function  $V_{t}^{\pi}$  is defined similarly. With the above notation, in fact, we can define it as

$$
V _ {t} ^ {\pi} (s) = Q _ {t} ^ {\pi} (s, \pi_ {t} (s _ {t}; s _ {1}, a _ {1}, \ldots , s _ {t - 1}, a _ {t - 1})).
$$

That the "Bellman equations" (3.2) hold is a direct consequence of these definitions. It also follows that when the values in (3.1) are well-defined, they agree with the definitions given here (regardless of the choice of the initial distribution used in the definitions in (3.1)).

# B Proof of Theorems in Section 4

In this section we prove Theorem 4.1 and Corollary 4.2.

# B.1 Proof of Theorem 4.1

We follow the proof in Dani et al. (2008) with a refined analysis. Let us start with recalling two well known results that we will need:

Lemma B.1 (Freedman 1975). Let  $M, v > 0$  be fixed constants. Let  $\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}$  be a stochastic process,  $\{\mathcal{G}_{i}\}_{i}$  be a filtration so that for all  $i \in [n]$ $x_{i}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ -measurable, while almost surely  $\mathbb{E}[x_{i}|\mathcal{G}_{i-1}] = 0$ ,  $|x_{i}| \leq M$  and

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {E} (x _ {i} ^ {2} | \mathcal {G} _ {i}) \leq v.
$$

Then, for any  $\delta > 0$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} x _ {i} \leq \sqrt {2 v \log (1 / \delta)} + 2 / 3 \cdot M \log (1 / \delta).
$$

Lemma B.2 (Lemma 11, Abbasi-Yadkori et al. 2011). For any  $\lambda > 0$  and sequence  $\{\mathbf{x}_t\}_{t=1}^T \subset \mathbb{R}^d$  for  $t \in \{0,1,\dots,T\}$ , define  $\mathbf{Z}_t = \lambda \mathbf{I} + \sum_{i=1}^t \mathbf{x}_i \mathbf{x}_i^\top$ . Then, provided that  $\|\mathbf{x}_t\|_2 \leq L$  holds for all  $t \in [T]$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right\} \leq 2 d \log \frac {d \lambda + T L ^ {2}}{d \lambda}.
$$

Recall that for  $t \geq 0$ ,  $\mathbf{Z}_t = \lambda \mathbf{I} + \sum_{i=1}^{t} \mathbf{x}_i \mathbf{x}_i^\top$ . Since  $\mathbf{Z}_t = \mathbf{Z}_{t-1} + \mathbf{x}_t \mathbf{x}_t^\top$ , by the matrix inversion lemma

$$
\mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} = \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} - \frac {\mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}}. \tag {B.1}
$$

We need the following definitions:

$$
\mathbf {d} _ {0} = 0, Z _ {0} = 0, \mathbf {d} _ {t} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i}, Z _ {t} = \left\| \mathbf {d} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1}}, w _ {t} = \left\| \mathbf {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}}, \mathcal {E} _ {t} = \mathbb {1} \{0 \leq s \leq t, Z _ {s} \leq \beta_ {s} \}, (B. 2)
$$

where  $t \geq 1$  and we define  $\beta_0 = 0$ . Recalling that  $x_{t}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_{t}$ -measurable and  $\eta_t$  is  $\mathcal{G}_{t+1}$ -measurable, we find that  $d_{t}, Z_{t}$  and  $\mathcal{E}_t$  are  $\mathcal{G}_{t+1}$ -measurable while  $w_{t}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_t$  measurable. We now prove the following result:

Lemma B.3. Let  $\mathbf{d}_i, w_i, \mathcal{E}_i$  be as defined in (B.2). Then, with probability at least  $1 - \delta / 2$ , simultaneously for all  $t \geq 1$  it holds that

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1} \leq 3 \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4.
$$

Proof. We have

$$
\left| \frac {2 \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1} \right| \leq \frac {2 \| \mathbf {x} _ {i} \| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1}} [ \| \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1} \| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1} ]}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \leq \frac {2 w _ {i} \beta_ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \leq \min  \{1, 2 w _ {i} \} \beta_ {i - 1}, \tag {B.3}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the second inequality holds due to the definition of  $\mathcal{E}_{i-1}$ , the last inequality holds by algebra. For simplicity, let  $\ell_i$  denote

$$
\ell_ {i} = \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1}. \tag {B.4}
$$

We are preparing to apply Freedman's inequality from Lemma B.1 to  $(\ell_i)_i$  and  $(\mathcal{G}_i)_i$ . First note that  $\mathbb{E}[\ell_i|\mathcal{G}_i] = 0$ . Meanwhile, by (B.3), the inequalities

$$
\left| \ell_ {i} \right| \leq R \beta_ {i - 1} \min  \{1, 2 w _ {i} \} \leq R \beta_ {i - 1} \leq R \beta_ {t} \tag {B.5}
$$

almost surely hold (the last inequality follows since  $(\beta_{i})_{i}$  is increasing). We also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {i} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right] \leq \sigma^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \left(\frac {2 \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \sigma^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \left[ \min  \left\{1, 2 w _ {i} \right\} \beta_ {i - 1} \right] ^ {2} \\ \leq 4 \sigma^ {2} \beta_ {t} ^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \min  \{1, w _ {i} ^ {2} \} \\ \leq 8 \sigma^ {2} \beta_ {t} ^ {2} d \log \left(1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)\right), \tag {B.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $\mathbb{E}[\eta_i^2 |\mathcal{G}_i]\leq \sigma^2$ , the second inequality holds due to (B.3), the third inequality holds again since  $(\beta_{i})_{i}$  is increasing, the last inequality holds due to Lemma B.2. Therefore, by (B.5) and (B.6), using Lemma B.1, we know that for any  $t$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta /(4t^{2})$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \ell_ {i} \leq \sqrt {1 6 \sigma^ {2} \beta_ {t} ^ {2} d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 2 / 3 \cdot R \beta_ {t} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) \\ \leq \frac {\beta_ {t} ^ {2}}{4} + 1 6 \sigma^ {2} d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \frac {\beta_ {t} ^ {2}}{4} + 4 R ^ {2} \log^ {2} (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) \\ \leq \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 2 + \frac {1}{4} \left(8 \sigma \sqrt {d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 R \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)\right) ^ {2} \\ = 3 \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4, \tag {B.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Lemma B.1, the second inequality holds due to  $2\sqrt{|ab|} \leq |a| + |b|$ , the last equality holds due to the definition of  $\beta_{t}$ . Taking union bound for (B.7) from  $t = 1$  to  $\infty$  and using the fact that  $\sum_{t=1}^{\infty} t^{-2} < 2$  finishes the proof.

We also need the following lemma.

Lemma B.4. Let  $w_{i}$  be as defined in (B.2). Then, with probability at least  $1 - \delta / 2$ , simultaneously for all  $t \geq 1$  it holds that

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \leq \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4.
$$

Proof. We are preparing to apply Freedman's inequality (Lemma B.1) to  $(\ell_i)_i$  and  $(\mathcal{G}_i)_i$  where now

$$
\ell_ {i} = \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} - \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right]. \tag {B.8}
$$

Clearly, for any  $i$ , we have  $\mathbb{E}[\ell_i|\mathcal{G}_i] = 0$  almost surely (a.s.). We further have that a.s.

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \ell_ {i} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right] \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {4} w _ {i} ^ {4}}{\left(1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}\right) ^ {2}} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq R ^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right] \\ \leq R ^ {2} \sigma^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \\ \leq 2 R ^ {2} \sigma^ {2} d \log \left(1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)\right), \tag {B.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to the fact  $\mathbb{E}(X - \mathbb{E}X)^2\leq \mathbb{E}X^2$ , the second inequality holds since  $|\eta_t|\leq R$  a.s., the third inequality holds since  $\mathbb{E}[\eta_i^2 |\mathcal{G}_i]\leq \sigma^2$  a.s. and  $w_{i}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_i$ -measurable, the fourth inequality holds due to the fact  $w_{i}^{2} / (1 + w_{i}^{2})\leq \min \{1,w_{i}^{2}\}$  and Lemma B.2. Furthermore, by the fact that  $|\eta_i|\leq R$  a.s., we have

$$
\left| \ell_ {i} \right| \leq \left| \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \right| + \left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right] \right| \leq 2 R ^ {2} \text {a . s .} \tag {B.10}
$$

Therefore, by (B.9) and (B.10), using Lemma B.1, we know that for any  $t$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta/(4t^2)$ , we have that a.s.,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {E} \left[ \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \Bigg | \mathcal {G} _ {i} \right] + \sqrt {4 R ^ {2} \sigma^ {2} d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 / 3 \cdot R ^ {2} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) \\ \leq \sigma^ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} + 2 R \sigma \sqrt {d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 2 R ^ {2} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) \\ \leq 2 \sigma^ {2} d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) + 2 R \sigma \sqrt {d \log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 2 R ^ {2} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) \\ \leq 1 / 4 \cdot \left(8 \sigma \sqrt {d} \sqrt {\log (1 + t L ^ {2} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 R \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)\right) ^ {2} \\ = \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4, \tag {B.11} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Lemma B.1, the second inequality holds due to  $\mathbb{E}[\eta_i^2 |\mathcal{G}_i]\leq \sigma^2$ , the third inequality holds due to the fact  $w_{i}^{2} / (1 + w_{i}^{2})\leq \min \{1,w_{i}^{2}\}$  and Lemma B.2, the last inequality holds due to the definition of  $\beta_{t}$ . Taking union bound for (B.11) from  $t = 1$  to  $\infty$  and using the fact that  $\sum_{t = 1}^{\infty}t^{-2} < 2$  finishes the proof.

With this, we are ready to prove Theorem 4.1.

Proof of Theorem 4.1. We first give a crude upper bound on  $Z_{t}$ . We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} Z _ {t} ^ {2} = \left(\mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {x} _ {t} \eta_ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} + \mathbf {x} _ {t} \eta_ {t}\right) \\ = \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} + 2 \eta_ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} + \eta_ {t} ^ {2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \\ \leq Z _ {t - 1} ^ {2} + \underbrace {2 \eta_ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1}} _ {I _ {1}} + \underbrace {\eta_ {t} ^ {2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t}} _ {I _ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the inequality holds since  $\mathbf{Z}_t\succeq \mathbf{Z}_{t - 1}$ . For term  $I_{1}$ , from the matrix inversion lemma (cf. (B.1)), we have

$$
I _ {1} = 2 \eta_ {t} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} - \frac {\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}}\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = 2 \eta_ {t} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1} - \frac {w _ {t} ^ {2} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}}\right) \\ = \frac {2 \eta_ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t - 1}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For term  $I_{2}$ , again from the matrix inversion lemma (cf. (B.1)), we have

$$
I _ {2} = \eta_ {t} ^ {2} \bigg (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \frac {\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}} \bigg) = \eta_ {t} ^ {2} \bigg (w _ {t} ^ {2} - \frac {w _ {t} ^ {4}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}} \bigg) = \frac {\eta_ {t} ^ {2} w _ {t} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {t} ^ {2}}.
$$

Therefore, we have

$$
Z _ {t} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}}. \tag {B.12}
$$

Consider now the event  $\mathcal{E}$  where the conclusions of Lemma B.3 and Lemma B.4 hold. We claim that on this event for any  $i\geq 0$ ,  $Z_{i}\leq \beta_{i}$ . We prove this by induction on  $i$ . Let the said event hold. The base case of  $i = 0$  holds since  $\beta_0 = 0 = Z_0$ , by definition. Now fix some  $t\geq 1$  and assume that for all  $0\leq i < t$ , we have  $Z_{i}\leq \beta_{i}$ . This implies that  $\mathcal{E}_1 = \mathcal{E}_2 = \dots = \mathcal{E}_{t - 1} = 1$ . Then by (B.12), we have

$$
Z _ {t} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1} + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}}. \tag {B.13}
$$

Since on the event  $\mathcal{E}$  the conclusions of Lemma B.3 and Lemma B.4 hold, we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {2 \eta_ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {Z} _ {i - 1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {i - 1}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \mathcal {E} _ {i - 1} \leq 3 \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4, \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \frac {\eta_ {i} ^ {2} w _ {i} ^ {2}}{1 + w _ {i} ^ {2}} \leq \beta_ {t} ^ {2} / 4. \tag {B.14}
$$

Therefore, substituting (B.14) into (B.13), we have  $Z_{t} \leq \beta_{t}$ , which ends the induction. Taking the union bound, the events in Lemma B.3 and Lemma B.4 hold with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , which implies that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , for any  $t$ ,  $Z_{t} \leq \beta_{t}$ .

Finally, we bound  $\| \pmb{\mu}_t - \pmb{\mu}^*\|_{\mathbf{Z}_t}$  as follows. First,

$$
\boldsymbol {\mu} _ {t} = \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {b} _ {t} = \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} + \eta_ {i}\right) = \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} - \lambda \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} + \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {d} _ {t}.
$$

Then, on  $\mathcal{E}$  we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t}} = \left\| \mathbf {d} _ {t} - \lambda \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \leq Z _ {t} + \sqrt {\lambda} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {2} \leq \beta_ {t} + \sqrt {\lambda} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {2}, \tag {B.15}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to triangle inequality and  $\mathbf{Z}_t\succeq \lambda \mathbf{I}$ , while the last one holds since we have shown that on  $\mathcal{E}$ ,  $Z_{t}\leq \beta_{t}$  for all  $t\geq 0$ , thus finishing the proof.

□

# B.2 Proof of Theorem 4.2

Proof of Theorem 4.2. By the assumption on  $\epsilon_t$ , we know that

$$
| \epsilon_ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} | \leq R / \bar {\sigma} _ {\min} ^ {t}, \mathbb {E} [ \epsilon_ {t} | \mathbf {a} _ {1: t}, \epsilon_ {1: t - 1} ] = 0, \mathbb {E} [ (\epsilon_ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t}) ^ {2} | \mathbf {a} _ {1: t}, \epsilon_ {1: t - 1} ] \leq 1, \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {2} \leq A / \bar {\sigma} _ {\min} ^ {t},
$$

Then, taking  $\mathcal{G}_t = \sigma (\mathbf{a}_{1:t},\epsilon_{1:t - 1})$ , using that  $\sigma_t$  is  $\mathcal{G}_t$ -measurable, we can apply Theorem 4.1 to  $(\boldsymbol{x}_t,\eta_t) = (\boldsymbol {a}_t / \sigma_t,\epsilon_t / \sigma_t)$  to get that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\forall t \geq 1, \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t}} \leq \widehat {\beta} _ {t} + \sqrt {\lambda} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {2} \leq \widehat {\beta} _ {t} + \sqrt {\lambda} B, \tag {B.16}
$$

where  $\widehat{\beta}_{t} = 8\sqrt{d\log(1 + tA^{2} / ([\bar{\sigma}_{\min}^{t}]^{2}d\lambda))\log(4t^{2} / \delta)} + 4R / \bar{\sigma}_{\min}^{t}\cdot \log (4t^{2} / \delta)$ . Thus, in the remainder of the proof, we will assume that the event  $\mathcal{E}$  when (B.16) is true holds and proceed to bound the regret on this event.

Note that on  $\mathcal{E}$ ,  $\pmb{\mu}^{*} \in \mathcal{C}_{t}$ . Recall that  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}_{t}$  is the optimistic parameter choice of the algorithm (cf. Line 4 in Algorithm 1). Then, using the standard argument for linear bandits, the pseudo-regret for round  $t$  is bounded by

$$
\langle \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle - \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle \leq \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t} \rangle - \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle = \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} \rangle + \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle , \tag {B.17}
$$

where the inequality holds due to the choice  $\widetilde{\mu}_t$ . To further bound (B.17), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} \right\rangle + \left\langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\rangle \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \left(\left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\mu}} _ {t - 1} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1}}\right) \\ \leq 2 \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}}, \tag {B.18} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the second one holds since  $\widetilde{\pmb{\mu}}_t,\pmb {\mu}^*\in \mathcal{C}_{t - 1}$ . Meanwhile, we have  $0\leq \langle \mathbf{a}_t^*,\pmb {\mu}^*\rangle -\langle \mathbf{a}_t,\pmb {\mu}^*\rangle \leq 2$ . Thus, substituting (B.18) into (B.17) and summing up (B.17) for  $t = 1,\ldots ,T$ , we have

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} (T) = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle - \langle \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \rangle \right] \leq 2 \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\}. \tag {B.19}
$$

To further bound the right-hand side above, we decompose the set  $[T]$  into a union of two disjoint subsets  $[T] = \mathcal{I}_1 \cup \mathcal{I}_2$ , where

$$
\mathcal {I} _ {1} = \left\{t \in [ T ]: \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \geq 1 \right\}, \mathcal {I} _ {2} = [ T ] \backslash \mathcal {I} _ {1}. \tag {B.20}
$$

Then the following upper bound of  $|\mathcal{I}_1|$  holds:

$$
\left| \mathcal {I} _ {1} \right| \leq \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {1}} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right\} \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right\} \leq 2 d \log \left(1 + T A ^ {2} / \left(d \lambda \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {\min } ^ {T} \right] ^ {2}\right)\right), \tag {B.21}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $\| \mathbf{a}_t / \bar{\sigma}_t\|_{\mathbf{A}_{t - 1}^{-1}}\geq 1$  for  $t\in \mathcal{I}_1$ , the third inequality holds due to Lemma B.2 together with the fact  $\| \mathbf{a}_t / \bar{\sigma}_t\| _2\leq A / \bar{\sigma}_{\mathrm{min}}^T$ . Therefore, by (B.19),

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} (T) / 2 =
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {1}} \min  \left\{1, \bar {\sigma} _ {t} (\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B) \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\} + \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {2}} \min  \left\{1, \bar {\sigma} _ {t} (\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B) \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\} \\ \leq \left[ \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {1}} 1 \right] + \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {2}} (\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B) \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \\ = \left| \mathcal {I} _ {1} \right| + \sum_ {t \in \mathcal {I} _ {2}} \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\} \\ \leq 2 d \log \left(1 + T A ^ {2} / \left(d \lambda \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {\min } ^ {T} \right] ^ {2}\right)\right) + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \min  \left\{1, \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\}, \tag {B.22} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since for any  $x$  real,  $\min \{1,x\} \leq 1$  and also  $\min \{1,x\} \leq x$ , the second inequality holds since  $\| \mathbf{a}_t / \bar{\sigma}_t\|_{\mathbf{A}_{t - 1}^{-1}}\leq 1$  for  $t\in \mathcal{I}_2$  and the last one holds due to (B.21). Finally, to further bound (B.22), notice that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \min  \left\{1, \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} \right\} \\ \leq \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} (\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B) ^ {2} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2}} \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1 , \| \mathbf {a} _ {t} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\mathbf {A} _ {t - 1} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(\widehat {\beta} _ {t - 1} + \sqrt {\lambda} B\right) ^ {2} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 d \log \left(1 + T A ^ {2} / \left(d \lambda \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {\min } ^ {T} \right] ^ {2}\right)\right)}, \tag {B.23} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the second one holds due to Lemma B.2 and the fact that  $\| \mathbf{a}_t / \sigma_t\| _2\leq A / \bar{\sigma}_{\min}^T$ . Substituting (B.23) into (B.22) yields our result.

# B.3 Proof of Corollary 4.3

Proof of Corollary 4.3. Since  $\bar{\sigma}_t = \max \{R / \sqrt{d},\sigma_t\}$ , then we have  $\bar{\sigma}_{\mathrm{min}}^t\geq R / \sqrt{d}$ . Therefore, with  $\lambda = 1 / B^{2}$ , we have

$$
\log \left(1 + T A ^ {2} / \left(d \lambda \left[ \bar {\sigma} _ {\min } ^ {T} \right] ^ {2}\right)\right) \leq \log \left(1 + T B ^ {2} A ^ {2} / R ^ {2}\right) = \widetilde {O} (1), \tag {B.24}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \widehat {\beta_ {t}} + \sqrt {\lambda} B = 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + t A ^ {2} / ([ \bar {\sigma} _ {\min} ^ {t} ] ^ {2} d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 R / \bar {\sigma} _ {\min} ^ {t} \cdot \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B \\ \leq 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + T B ^ {2} A ^ {2} / R ^ {2}) \log (4 T ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 T ^ {2} / \delta) + 1 \\ = \widetilde {O} (\sqrt {d}). \tag {B.25} \\ \end{array}
$$

Substituting (B.24) and (B.25) into (4.8), we have

$$
\mathrm {R e g r e t} (T) = \widetilde {O} \bigg (d \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2}} \bigg) = \widetilde {O} \bigg (d \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} (R ^ {2} / d + \sigma_ {t} ^ {2})} \bigg) = \widetilde {O} \bigg (R \sqrt {d T} + d \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \sigma_ {t} ^ {2}} \bigg),
$$

where the second equality holds since  $\bar{\sigma}_t^2 = \max \{R^2 /d,\sigma_t^2\} \leq R^2 /d + \sigma_t^2$  , the third equality holds since  $\sqrt{|x| + |y|}\leq \sqrt{|x|} +\sqrt{|y|}$

# B.4 Derivation of the Bound in (4.5)

In this subsection, we derive the bound in (4.5) by the concentration inequality proved in Faury et al. (2020). The following proposition is a restatement of Theorem 1 in Faury et al. (2020).

Proposition B.5 (Theorem 1, Faury et al. 2020). Let  $\{\mathcal{G}_t\}_{t=1}^{\infty}$  be a filtration, where  $\mathbf{x}_t \in \mathbb{R}^d$  is  $\mathcal{G}_t$ -measurable and  $\eta_t \in \mathbb{R}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_{t+1}$ -measurable. Suppose  $\eta_t, \mathbf{x}_t$  satisfy that

$$
\left| \eta_ {t} \right| \leq 1, \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta_ {t} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {t} \right] = 0, \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta_ {t} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {t} \right] \leq \sigma_ {t} ^ {2}, \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} \leq 1,
$$

Let  $\mathbf{H}_t = \lambda \mathbf{I} + \sum_{i=1}^t \sigma_t^2 \mathbf{x}_i \mathbf{x}_i^\top$ . Then for any  $0 < \delta < 1$ ,  $\lambda > 0$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$  we have

$$
\forall t > 0, \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i} \right\| _ {\mathbf {H} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \leq \frac {\sqrt {\lambda}}{2} + \frac {2}{\sqrt {\lambda}} \log \left(\frac {\det  \left(\mathbf {H} _ {t}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \lambda^ {- d / 2}}{\delta}\right) + \frac {2 d \log 2}{\sqrt {\lambda}}. \tag {B.26}
$$

In the following, we first extend the above bound to the general case, where  $|\eta_t| \leq R$ ,  $\mathbb{E}[\eta_t^2 |\mathcal{G}_t] \leq \sigma^2$ ,  $\| \mathbf{x}_t\|_2 \leq L$ . In specific, we have

$$
\left| \eta_ {t} / R \right| \leq 1, \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta_ {t} / R \mid \mathcal {G} _ {t} \right] = 0, \mathbb {E} \left[ \eta_ {t} ^ {2} / R ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {G} _ {t} \right] \leq \sigma^ {2} / R ^ {2}, \| \mathbf {x} _ {t} / L \| _ {2} \leq 1,
$$

Therefore, by Proposition B.5, let

$$
\bar {\mathbf {H}} _ {t} = \lambda \mathbf {I} + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \sigma^ {2} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} / (R ^ {2} L ^ {2}),
$$

the following holds with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \forall t > 0, \left\| \bar {\mathbf {H}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i} / (R L) \right\| _ {2} \leq \frac {\sqrt {\lambda}}{2} + \frac {2}{\sqrt {\lambda}} \log \left(\det (\bar {\mathbf {H}} _ {t}) ^ {1 / 2} \lambda^ {- d / 2}\right) + \frac {2 d \log 2 + 2 \log (1 / \delta)}{\sqrt {\lambda}} \\ \leq \frac {\sqrt {\lambda}}{2} + \frac {d}{\sqrt {\lambda}} \log \left(1 + t \sigma^ {2} / \lambda\right) + \frac {2 d \log 2 + 2 \log (1 / \delta)}{\sqrt {\lambda}}, \tag {B.27} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds since  $\operatorname{det}(\bar{\mathbf{H}}_t) \leq \| \bar{\mathbf{H}}_t\|_2^d \leq (\lambda + t\sigma^2)^d$ . Set  $\lambda \gets \lambda \sigma^2 / (R^2 L^2)$ , then (B.27) becomes

$$
\forall t > 0, \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \leq \sigma \left(\frac {\sigma \sqrt {\lambda}}{2 R L} + \frac {d R L}{\sigma \sqrt {\lambda}} \log \left(1 + t R ^ {2} L ^ {2} / \lambda\right) + \frac {2 d \log 2 + 2 \log (1 / \delta)}{\sigma \sqrt {\lambda}} R L\right), \tag {B.28}
$$

Now we are going to bound  $\| \pmb{\mu}_t - \pmb{\mu}^*\|_{\mathbf{Z}_t}$  by (B.28). By the definition of  $\pmb{\mu}_t$ , we have

$$
\boldsymbol {\mu} _ {t} = \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {b} _ {t} = \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} + \eta_ {i}\right) = \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} - \lambda \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} + \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i},
$$

then  $\| \pmb{\mu}_t - \pmb{\mu}^*\|_{\mathbf{Z}_t}$  can be bounded as

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t}} = \left\| \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i} + \lambda \mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\| _ {2} \leq \left\| \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {t} \mathbf {x} _ {i} \eta_ {i} \right\| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} + \sqrt {\lambda} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {2}, \tag {B.29}
$$

where the first equality holds due to triangle inequality and  $\mathbf{Z}_t\succeq \lambda \mathbf{I}$ . Next, substituting (B.28) into (B.29) yields

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {\mathbf {Z} _ {t}} \leq \frac {\sigma^ {2} \sqrt {\lambda}}{2 R L} + \frac {d R L}{\sqrt {\lambda}} \log (1 + t R ^ {2} L ^ {2} / \lambda) + \frac {2 d \log 2 + 2 \log (1 / \delta)}{\sqrt {\lambda}} R L + \sqrt {\lambda} \| \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \| _ {2}.
$$

Finally, set  $\lambda = \widetilde{\Theta} (dR^2 L^2 /(\sigma^2 +RL\| \pmb {\mu}^*\| _2))$  to minimize the above upper bound, we have  $\| \pmb {\mu}_t - \pmb {\mu}^*\|_{\mathbf{Z}_t}\leq \tilde{O} (\sigma \sqrt{d} +\sqrt{dRL\| \pmb{\mu}^*\|_2})$

# C Proof of Main Results in Section 5

Further, we let  $\mathbb{P}$  be the distribution over  $(\mathcal{S} \times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$  induced by the interconnection of UCRL-VTR $^+$  (treated as a nonstationary, history dependent policy) and the episodic MDP  $M$ . Further, let  $\mathbb{E}$  be the corresponding expectation operator. Note that the only source of randomness are the stochastic transitions in the MDP, hence, all random variables can be defined over the sample space  $\Omega = (\mathcal{S} \times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$ . Thus, we work with the probability space given by the triplet  $(\Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P})$ , where  $\mathcal{F}$  is the product  $\sigma$ -algebra generated by the discrete  $\sigma$ -algebras underlying  $\mathcal{S}$  and  $\mathcal{A}$ , respectively.

For  $1 \leq k \leq K$ ,  $1 \leq h \leq H$ , let  $\mathcal{F}_{k,h}$  be the  $\sigma$ -algebra generated by the random variables representing the state-action pairs up to and including those that appear stage  $h$  of episode  $k$ . That is,  $\mathcal{F}_{k,h}$  is generated by

$$
s _ {1} ^ {1}, a _ {1} ^ {1}, \ldots , s _ {h} ^ {1}, a _ {h} ^ {1}, \ldots , s _ {H} ^ {1}, a _ {H} ^ {1},
$$

$$
s _ {1} ^ {2}, a _ {1} ^ {2}, \ldots , s _ {h} ^ {2}, a _ {h} ^ {2}, \ldots , s _ {H} ^ {2}, a _ {H} ^ {2},
$$

![](images/5c343227b77de9a5fd74abc6529a18f9edf8cc797cd8aa3726f9ade6ff792601.jpg)

$$
s _ {1} ^ {k}, a _ {1} ^ {k}, \ldots , s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}.
$$

Note that, by construction,

$$
\bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}), E _ {k, h}, \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h}, \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k + 1, h}, \widetilde {\Sigma} _ {k + 1, h},
$$

are  $\mathcal{F}_{k,h}$ -measurable,  $\widehat{\pmb{b}}_{k+1,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{b}}_{k+1,h}, \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k+1,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{k+1,h}$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{k,h+1}$ -measurable, and  $Q_{k,h}, V_{k,h}, \pi_h^k, \phi_{V_{k,h+1}}$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{k-1,H}$ -measurable. Note also that  $Q_{k,h}, V_{k,h}, \pi_h^k, \phi_{V_{k,h+1}}$  are not  $\mathcal{F}_{k-1,h}$ -measurable: The get their values only after episode  $k-1$  is over, due to their "backwards" construction.

# C.1 Proof of Lemma 5.2

The main idea of the proof is to use a (crude) two-step, "peeling" device. Let  $\check{C}_{k,h},\widetilde{C}_{k,h}$  denote the following confidence sets:

$$
\check {\mathcal {C}} _ {k, h} = \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta}: \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \check {\beta} _ {k} \right\},
$$

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {k, h} = \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta}: \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \widetilde {\beta} _ {k} \right\}.
$$

Note that  $\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h} \subset \check{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ : The "leading term" in the definition of  $\check{\beta}_k$  is larger than that in  $\widehat{\beta}_k$  by a factor of  $\sqrt{d}$ . The idea of our proof is to show that  $\pmb{\theta}_{h}^{*}$  is included in  $\check{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h} \cap \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  with high probability

(for this, a standard self-normalized tail inequality suffices) and then use that when this holds, the weights used in constructing  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h}$  are sufficiently precise to "balance" the noise term, which allows to reduce  $\check{\beta}_k$  by the extra  $\sqrt{d}$  factor without significantly increasing the probability of the bad event when  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \notin \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ .

We start with the following lemma.

Lemma C.1. Let  $V_{k,h + 1},\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h},\widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h},\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h},\widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h}$  be defined in Algorithm 2, then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right| \\ \leq \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ + \min \Big \{H ^ {2}, 2 H \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \right\| _ {2} \Big \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \big (\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \big) \Big \| _ {2} \Big \}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right| \\ = \left| \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, H ^ {2} ]} - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right. \\ + \left(\left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle\right) ^ {2} - \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, H ]} ^ {2} \\ \leq \underbrace {\left| \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) , \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k , h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0 , H ^ {2} ]} - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) , \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right|} _ {I _ {1}} \\ + \underbrace {\left| \left(\left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) , \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle\right) ^ {2} - \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) , \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k , h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0 , H ]} ^ {2} \right|} _ {I _ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the inequality holds due to the triangle inequality. We bound  $I_{1}$  first. We have  $I_{1} \leq H^{2}$  since both terms in  $I_{1}$  belong to the interval  $[0, H^{2}]$ . Furthermore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {1} \leq \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right| \\ = \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $\langle \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}^2}(s_h^k,a_h^k),\pmb{\theta}_h^*\rangle \in [0,H^2 ]$  and the second inequality holds due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Thus, we have

$$
I _ {1} \leq \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \| _ {2} \}. \tag {C.1}
$$

For the term  $I_{2}$ , since both terms in  $I_{2}$  belong to the interval  $[0, H^{2}]$ , we have  $I_{2} \leq H^{2}$ . Meanwhile,

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {2} = \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle + \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, H ]} \right| \\ \cdot \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle - \left[ \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right] _ {[ 0, H ]} \right| \\ \leq 2 H \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right| \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = 2 H \left| \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq 2 H \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2}, \tag {C.2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since both terms in this line are less than  $H$  and the fact  $\langle \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}}(s_h^k,a_h^k),\pmb{\theta}_h^*\rangle \in [0,H]$ , the second inequality holds due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Thus, we have

$$
I _ {2} \leq \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, 2 H \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\}. \tag {C.3}
$$

Combining (C.1) and (C.3) gives the desired result.

Proof of Lemma 5.2. Fix  $h \in [H]$ . We first show that with probability at least  $1 - \delta / H$ , for all  $k$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \check{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ . To show this, we apply Theorem 4.1. Let  $\mathbf{x}_i = \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}\phi_{V_{i,h+1}}(s_h^i, a_h^i)$  and  $\eta_i = \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}V_{i,h+1}(s_{h+1}^i) - \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}\langle\phi_{V_{i,h+1}}(s_{i,h}, a_{i,h}), \pmb{\theta}_h^*\rangle$ ,  $\mathcal{G}_i = \mathcal{F}_{i,h}$ ,  $\pmb{\mu}^* = \pmb{\theta}_h^*$ ,  $y_i = \langle\pmb{\mu}^*, \mathbf{x}_i\rangle + \eta_i$ ,  $\mathbf{Z}_i = \lambda \mathbf{I} + \sum_{i'=1}^{i} \mathbf{x}_{i'}\mathbf{x}_{i'}^\top$ ,  $\mathbf{b}_i = \sum_{i'=1}^{i} \mathbf{x}_{i'}y_{i'}$  and  $\pmb{\mu}_i = \mathbf{Z}_i^{-1}\mathbf{b}_i$ . Then it can be verified that  $y_i = \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}V_{i,h+1}(s_{h+1}^i)$  and  $\pmb{\mu}_i = \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{i+1,h}$ . Moreover, almost surely,

$$
\| \mathbf {x} _ {i} \| _ {2} \leq \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1} H \leq \sqrt {d}, | \eta_ {i} | \leq \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1} H \leq \sqrt {d}, \mathbb {E} [ \eta_ {i} | \mathcal {G} _ {i} ] = 0, \mathbb {E} [ \eta_ {i} ^ {2} | \mathcal {G} _ {i} ] \leq d,
$$

where we used that  $V_{i,h+1}$  takes values in  $[0,H]$  and that  $\| \phi_{V_{i,h+1}}(s,a) \|_2 \leq H$  by (3.3). Since we also have that  $\pmb{x}_i$  is  $\mathcal{G}_i$  measurable and  $\eta_i$  is  $\mathcal{G}_{i+1}$  measurable, by Theorem 4.1, we obtain that with probability at least  $1 - \delta / H$ , for all  $k \leq K$ ,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h}} \leq 8 d \sqrt {\log (1 + k / \lambda) \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \check {\beta} _ {k}, \tag {C.4}
$$

implying that with probability  $1 - \delta / H$ , for any  $k \leq K$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \mathcal{C}_{k,h}$ .

An argument, which is analogous to the one just used (except that now the range of the "noise" matches the range of "squared values" and is thus bounded by  $H^2$ , rather than being bounded by  $\sqrt{d}$ ) gives that with probability at least  $1 - \delta / H$ , for any  $k \leq K$  we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h}} \leq 8 \sqrt {d H ^ {4} \log (1 + k H ^ {4} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 H ^ {2} \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\beta} _ {k}, \tag {C.5}
$$

which implies that with the said probability,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ .

We now show that  $\theta_h^* \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  with high probability. We again apply Theorem 4.1. Let  $\mathbf{x}_i = \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}\phi_{V_{i,h+1}}(s_h^i, a_h^i)$  and

$$
\eta_ {i} = \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {1} \{\pmb {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \in \check {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \} \big [ V _ {i, h + 1} (s _ {h + 1} ^ {i}) - \langle \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1}} (s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}), \pmb {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \rangle \big ],
$$

$\mathcal{G}_i = \mathcal{F}_{i,h}$ ,  $\pmb{\mu}^{*} = \pmb{\theta}_{h}^{*}$ . Clearly  $\mathbb{E}[\eta_i|\mathcal{G}_i] = 0$ ,  $|\eta_i| \leq \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}H \leq \sqrt{d}$  since  $|V_{i,h+1}(\cdot)| \leq H$  and  $\bar{\sigma}_{i,h} \geq H / \sqrt{d}$ ,  $\| \mathbf{x}_i \|_2 \leq \bar{\sigma}_{i,h}^{-1}H \leq \sqrt{d}$ . Furthermore, owning to that  $\mathbb{1}\{\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \tilde{\mathcal{C}}_{i,h} \cap \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{i,h}\}$  is  $\mathcal{G}_i$ -measurable, it holds that

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \eta_ {i} ^ {2} | \mathcal {G} _ {i} ] = \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 2} \mathbb {1} \{\pmb {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \in \check {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \} [ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {i, h + 1} ] (s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i})
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 2} \mathbb {1} \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \in \check {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \right\} \left[ \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {i, h} V _ {i, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right. \\ + \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {i, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ \left. + \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, 2 H \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {i, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \right] \\ \leq \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 2} \left[ \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {i, h} V _ {i, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) + \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, \widetilde {\beta} _ {i} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {V _ {i, h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \right. \\ \left. + \min  \left\{H ^ {2}, 2 H \check {\beta} _ {i} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \right] \\ = 1, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Lemma C.1, the second inequality holds due to the indicator function, the last equality holds due to the definition of  $\bar{\sigma}_{i,h}$ . Now, let  $y_{i} = \langle \pmb{\mu}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle +\eta_{i}$ ,  $\mathbf{Z}_i = \lambda \mathbf{I} + \sum_{i' = 1}^{i}\mathbf{x}_{i'}\mathbf{x}_{i'}^\top$ ,  $\mathbf{b}_i = \sum_{i' = 1}^{i}\mathbf{x}_{i'}y_{i'}$  and  $\pmb{\mu}_i = \mathbf{Z}_i^{-1}\mathbf{b}_i$ . Then, by Theorem 4.1, with probability at least  $1 - \delta /H$ ,  $\forall k\leq K$ ,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\mathbf {z} _ {i}} \leq 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + k / \lambda) \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widehat {\beta} _ {k}, \tag {C.6}
$$

where the equality uses the definition of  $\widehat{\beta}_k$ . Let  $\mathcal{E}'$  be the event when  $\theta_h^* \in \cap_{k \leq K} \check{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h} \cap \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  and (C.6) hold. By the union bound,  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}') \geq 1 - 3\delta / H$ .

We now show that  $\pmb{\theta}_{h}^{*}\in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  holds on  $\mathcal{E}'$ . For this note that on  $\mathcal{E}'$ , for all  $k\leq K$ ,  $\pmb{\mu}_k = \widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k + 1,h}$  for any  $k\leq K$ . Indeed, on this event, for any  $i\leq K$

$$
\begin{array}{l} y _ {i} = \bar {\sigma} _ {i, h} ^ {- 1} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right) \right\rangle + \mathbb {1} \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \in \check {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {i, h} \right\} \left[ V _ {i, h + 1} \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {i}\right) - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {i, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {i}, a _ {h} ^ {i}\right), \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \right\rangle \right]\right) \\ = V _ {i, h + 1} \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {i}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which does implies the claim. Therefore, by the definition of  $\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  and since on  $\mathcal{E}'$  (C.6) holds, we get that on  $\mathcal{E}'$ , the relation  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$  also holds. Finally, taking union bound over  $h$  and substituting (C.4) and (C.5) into Lemma C.1 shows that with probability at least  $1 - 3\delta$ ,

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \in \cap_ {k, h} \widehat {\mathcal {C}} _ {k, h} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {k, h} \tag {C.7}
$$

To finish our proof, it is thus sufficient to show that on the event when (C.7) holds, it also holds that

$$
\left| \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right| \leq E _ {k, h}.
$$

However, this is immediate from Lemma C.1 and the definition of  $E_{k,h}$ .

# C.2 Proof of Theorem 5.3

In this subsection we prove Theorem 5.3. The proof is broken down into a number of lemmas. However, first we need the Azuma-Hoeffding inequality:

Lemma C.2 (Azuma-Hoeffding inequality, Azuma 1967). Let  $M > 0$  be a constant. Let  $\{x_{i}\}_{i = 1}^{n}$  be a martingale difference sequence with respect to a filtration  $\{\mathcal{G}_i\}_i$  ( $\mathbb{E}[x_i|\mathcal{G}_i] = 0$  a.s. and  $x_{i}$  is

$\mathcal{G}_{i + 1}$ -measurable) such that for all  $i \in [n]$ ,  $|x_i| \leq M$  holds almost surely. Then, for any  $0 < \delta < 1$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} x _ {i} \leq M \sqrt {2 n \log (1 / \delta)}.
$$

For the remainder of this subsection, let  $\mathcal{E}$  denote the event when the conclusion of Lemma 5.2 holds. Then Lemma 5.2 suggests  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}) \geq 1 - 3\delta$ . We introduce another two events  $\mathcal{E}_1$  and  $\mathcal{E}_2$ :

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {1} = \left\{\forall h ^ {\prime} \in [ H ], \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = h ^ {\prime}} ^ {H} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left(V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}\right) \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right) \right] \leq 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)} \right\},
$$

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {2} = \Bigg \{\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} [ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \leq 3 (H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)) \Bigg \}.
$$

Then we have  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_1) \geq 1 - \delta$  and  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_2) \geq 1 - \delta$ . The first one holds since  $[\mathbb{P}_h(V_{k,h+1} - V_{h+1}^{\pi^k})](s_h^k, a_h^k) - [V_{k,h+1} - V_{h+1}^{\pi^k}](s_{h+1}^k)$  forms a martingale difference sequence and  $|[[\mathbb{P}_h(V_{k,h+1} - V_{h+1}^{\pi^k})](s_h^k, a_h^k) - [V_{k,h+1} - V_{h+1}^{\pi^k}](s_{h+1}^k)]| \leq 4H$ . Applying the Azuma-Hoeffding inequality (Lemma C.2), we find that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , simultaneously for all  $h' \in [H]$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = h ^ {\prime}} ^ {H} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left(V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}\right) \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right) \right] \leq 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}, \tag {C.8}
$$

which implies  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_1) \geq 1 - \delta$ . That  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_2) \geq 1 - \delta$  holds is due to the following lemma:

Lemma C.3 (Total variance lemma, Lemma C.5, Jin et al. 2018). With probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} [ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \leq 3 (H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)).
$$

We now prove the following three lemmas based on  $\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}_1,\mathcal{E}_2$

Lemma C.4. Let  $Q_{k,h}, V_{k,h}$  be defined in Algorithm 2. Then, on the event  $\mathcal{E}$ , for any  $s, a, k, h$  we have that  $Q_h^*(s,a) \leq Q_{k,h}(s,a)$ ,  $V_h^*(s) \leq V_{k,h}(s)$ .

Proof. Since  $\mathcal{E}$  holds, we have for any  $k\in [K]$  and  $h\in [H]$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^*\in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ . We prove the statement by induction. The statement holds for  $h = H + 1$  since  $Q_{k,H + 1}(\cdot ,\cdot) = 0 = Q_{H + 1}^{*}(\cdot ,\cdot)$ . Assume the statement holds for  $h + 1$ . That is,  $Q_{k,h + 1}(\cdot ,\cdot)\geq Q_{h + 1}^{*}(\cdot ,\cdot)$ ,  $V_{k,h + 1}(\cdot)\geq V_{h + 1}^{*}(\cdot)$ . Given  $s,a$ , if  $Q_{k,h}(s,a)\geq H$ , then  $Q_{k,h}(s,a)\geq H\geq Q_h^* (s,a)$ . Otherwise, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} Q _ {k, h} (s, a) - Q _ {h} ^ {*} (s, a) \\ = \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s, a), \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} \right\rangle + \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s, a) \right\| _ {2} - \left\langle \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s, a), \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*} \right\rangle \\ + \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} (s, a) - \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {*} (s, a) \\ \geq \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s, a) \right\| _ {2} - \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s, a) \right\| _ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} + \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} (s, a) - \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {*} (s, a) \\ \geq \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} (s, a) - \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {*} (s, a) \\ \geq 0, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz, the second inequality holds by the assumption that  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{k,h}$ , the third inequality holds by the induction assumption and because  $\mathbb{P}_h$  is a monotone operator with respect to the partial ordering of functions. Therefore, for all  $s,a$ , we have  $Q_{k,h}(s,a) \geq Q_h^*(s,a)$ , which implies  $V_{k,h}(s) \geq V_h^*(s)$ , finishing the inductive step and thus the proof.

Lemma C.5. Let  $V_{k,h},\bar{\sigma}_{k,h}$  be defined in Algorithm 2. Then, on the event  $\mathcal{E}\cap \mathcal{E}_1$  , we have

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left[ V _ {k, 1} (s _ {1} ^ {k}) - V _ {1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} (s _ {1} ^ {k}) \right] \leq 2 \widehat {\beta_ {K}} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 H d \log (1 + K / \lambda)} + 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)},
$$

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \mathbb {P} _ {h} [ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 d H ^ {3} \log (1 + K / \lambda)} + 4 H ^ {2} \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}.
$$

Proof. Assume that  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_1$  holds. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} V _ {k, h} (s _ {h} ^ {k}) - V _ {h} ^ {\pi^ {k}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}) \leq \langle \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h}, \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \rangle - [ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) + \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {k, h} - \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {h} ^ {*}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \\ + \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) + \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) + 2 \widehat {\beta_ {k}} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2}, \tag {C.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to the definition of  $V_{k,h}$  and the Bellman equation for  $V_h^{\pi^k}$ , the second inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and because we are in a linear MDP, the third inequality holds by the fact that on  $\mathcal{E}$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^* \in \widehat{C}_{k,h}$ . Meanwhile, since  $V_{k,h}(s_h^k) - V_h^{\pi^k}(s_h^k) \leq H$ , we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} V _ {k, h} (s _ {h} ^ {k}) - V _ {h} ^ {\pi^ {k}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}) \\ \leq \min  \left\{H, 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} + \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\} \\ \leq \min  \left\{H, 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} + \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \\ \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {2} \right\} + \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \tag {C.10} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds since the optimal value function dominates the value function of any policy, and thus on  $\mathcal{E}$ , by Lemma C.4,  $V_{k,h+1}(\cdot) \geq V_{h+1}^{\pi^k}(\cdot)$ , the third inequality holds since  $2\widehat{\beta}_k\bar{\sigma}_{k,h} \geq \sqrt{d} \cdot H / \sqrt{d} \geq H$ . By (C.10) we have

$$
V _ {k, h} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - V _ {h} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right) - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right) \right] \tag {C.11}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ + \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right). \tag {C.12} \\ \end{array}
$$

Summing up these inequalities for  $k \in [K]$  and  $h = h', \ldots, H$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left[ V _ {k, h ^ {\prime}} \left(s _ {k, h ^ {\prime}}\right) - V _ {h ^ {\prime}} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(s _ {k, h ^ {\prime}}\right) \right] \\ \leq 2 \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = h ^ {\prime}} ^ {H} \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = h ^ {\prime}} ^ {H} \left[ [ \mathbb {P} _ {h} (V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}) ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) - [ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}) \right] \\ \leq 2 \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \widehat {\beta} _ {k} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} \right\| _ {2} \right\}} _ {I _ {1}} + 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}, \tag {C.13} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds by a telescoping argument and since  $V_{k,H + 1}(\cdot) = V_{h + 1}^{\pi^k}(\cdot) = 0$ , the second inequality holds due to  $\mathcal{E}_1$ . To further bound  $I_{1}$ , we have

$$
