\begin{array}{l} I _ {1} \leq \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \widehat {\beta} _ {k} ^ {2} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k , h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 H d \log (1 + K / \lambda)}, \tag {C.14} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the second inequality holds since  $\widehat{\beta}_k\leq \widehat{\beta}_K$ , the third inequality holds due to Lemma B.2 with the fact that  $\| \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}}(s_h^k,a_h^k) / \bar{\sigma}_{k,h}\| _2\leq$ $\| \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}}(s_h^k,a_h^k)\| _2\cdot \sqrt{d} /H\leq \sqrt{d}$ . Substituting (C.14) into (C.13) gives

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left[ V _ {k, h ^ {\prime}} \left(s _ {k, h ^ {\prime}}\right) - V _ {h ^ {\prime}} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(s _ {k, h ^ {\prime}}\right) \right] \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 H d \log (1 + K / \lambda)} + 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}. \tag {C.15}
$$

Choosing  $h' = 1$  here we get the first inequality that was to be proven. To get the second inequality, note that

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 2} ^ {H} \left[ V _ {k, h} - V _ {h} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \\ + \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left(V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}\right) \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h + 1} ^ {k}\right) \right] \\ \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 d H ^ {3} \log (1 + K H / (d \lambda))} + 4 H ^ {2} \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where to get the last inequality we sum up (C.15) for  $h' = 2, \ldots, H$ , and use the inequality that defines  $\mathcal{E}_1$ , which is followed by loosening the resulting bound.

The next lemma is concerned with bounding  $\sum_{k=1}^{K} \sum_{h=1}^{H} \bar{\sigma}_{k,h}^2$  on  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_2$ :

Lemma C.6. Let  $V_{k,h}, \bar{\sigma}_{k,h}$  be defined in Algorithm 2. Then, on the event  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_2$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq H ^ {2} T / d + 3 (H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)) + 2 H \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \mathbb {P} _ {h} [ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \\ + 2 \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K H ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \breve {\beta} _ {K} H ^ {2} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K / \lambda)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. Assume that  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_2$  holds. Since we are on  $\mathcal{E}$ , by Lemma C.4, for all  $k,h$ ,  $V_{k,h}(\cdot) \geq V_h^*(\cdot) \geq V_h^{\pi^k}(\cdot)$ . Now, we calculate

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ H ^ {2} / d + \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) + E _ {k, h} \right] \\ = H ^ {2} T / d + \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {k , h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right]} _ {I _ {1}} + \underbrace {2 \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} E _ {k , h}} _ {I _ {2}} \\ + \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right)} _ {I _ {3}} + \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k , h} V _ {k , h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {V} _ {h} V _ {k , h + 1} \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) - E _ {k , h} \right]} _ {I _ {4}}, \tag {C.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to the definition of  $\bar{\sigma}_{k,h}$ . To bound  $I_{1}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {1} \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} [ \mathbb {P} _ {h} V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) - [ \mathbb {P} _ {h} (V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}) ^ {2} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \\ \leq 2 H \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {h} \left(V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}}\right) \right] \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $V_{h+1}^{\pi^k}(\cdot) \leq V_{h+1}^*(\cdot) \leq V_{k,h+1}(\cdot)$ , the second inequality holds since  $V_{h+1}^{\pi^k}(\cdot), V_{k,h+1}(\cdot) \leq H$ . To bound  $I_2$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {2} \leq 2 \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \widetilde {\beta} _ {k} \min \left\{1, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1} ^ {2}} (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ + 4 H \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \check {\beta} _ {k} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\Sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k, h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ \leq 2 \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k , h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ + 7 \check {\beta} _ {K} H ^ {2} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {k , h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {k , h + 1}} \left(s _ {h} ^ {k} , a _ {h} ^ {k}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq 2 \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K H ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \breve {\beta} _ {K} H ^ {2} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K / \lambda)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $\widetilde{\beta}_k\geq H^2$  and  $\breve{\beta}_k\bar{\sigma}_{k,h}\geq \sqrt{d}\cdot H / \sqrt{d} = H$ , the second inequality holds due to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,  $\widetilde{\beta}_k\leq \widetilde{\beta}_K$ ,  $\breve{\beta}_k\leq \breve{\beta}_K$ , and the following bound on  $\bar{\sigma}_{k,h}$  due to the definitions of  $\bar{\sigma}_{k,h}$ ,  $[\overline{\mathbb{V}}_{k,h}V_{k,h + 1}](s_h^k,a_h^k)$  and  $E_{k,h}$ :

$$
\bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {2} = \max  \left\{H ^ {2} / d, [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {k, h} V _ {k, h + 1} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) + E _ {k, h} \right\} \leq \max  \left\{H ^ {2} / d, H ^ {2} + 2 H ^ {2} \right\} = 3 H ^ {2}.
$$

Finally, the third inequality holds due to Lemma B.2 together with the facts that  $\left\| \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}^2}(s_h^k,a_h^k)\right\| _2\leq H^2$  and  $\left\| \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}}(s_h^k,a_h^k) / \bar{\sigma}_{k,h}\right\| _2\leq \left\| \phi_{V_{k,h + 1}}(s_h^k,a_h^k)\right\| _2\cdot \sqrt{d} /H\leq \sqrt{d}$ . To bound  $I_{3}$ , since  $\mathcal{E}_2$  holds, we have

$$
I _ {3} \leq 3 \left(H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)\right).
$$

Finally, due to Lemma 5.2, we have  $I_4 \leq 0$ . Substituting  $I_1, I_2, I_3, I_4$  into (C.16) ends our proof.

With all above lemmas, we are ready to prove Theorem 5.3.

Proof of Theorem 5.3. By construction, taking a union bound, we have with probability  $1 - 5\delta$  that  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_1 \cap \mathcal{E}_2$  holds. In the remainder of the proof, assume that we are on this event. Thus, we can also use the conclusions of Lemmas C.4, C.5 and C.6. We bound the regret as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {R e g r e t} \left(M _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}}, K\right) \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left[ V _ {k, 1} \left(s _ {1} ^ {k}\right) - V _ {1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(s _ {1} ^ {k}\right) \right] \\ \leq 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 H d \log (1 + K H / (d \lambda))} + 4 H \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)} \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(\sqrt {d H} \sqrt {d} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} + H \sqrt {T}\right), \tag {C.17} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Lemma C.4, the second inequality holds due to Lemma C.5, the equality holds since when  $\lambda = 1 / B^2$ ,

$$
\widehat {\beta} _ {K} = 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + K / \lambda) \log (4 K ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 K ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (\sqrt {d}).
$$

It remains to bound  $\sum_{k=1}^{K} \sum_{h=1}^{H} \bar{\sigma}_{k,h}^{2}$ . For this we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq H ^ {2} T / d + 3 (H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)) + 2 H \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \mathbb {P} _ {h} [ V _ {k, h + 1} - V _ {h + 1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} ] (s _ {h} ^ {k}, a _ {h} ^ {k}) \\ + 2 \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K H ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \breve {\beta} _ {K} H ^ {2} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K / \lambda)} \\ \leq H ^ {2} T / d + 3 \left(H T + H ^ {3} \log (1 / \delta)\right) + 2 H \\ \cdot \left(2 \widehat {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {2 d H ^ {3} \log (1 + K / \lambda)} + 4 H ^ {2} \sqrt {2 T \log (H / \delta)}\right) \\ + 2 \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K H ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \breve {\beta} _ {K} H ^ {2} \sqrt {T} \sqrt {2 d H \log (1 + K / \lambda)} \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(\sqrt {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k , h} ^ {2}} \sqrt {d ^ {2} H ^ {5}} + H ^ {2} T / d + T H + \sqrt {T} d ^ {1. 5} H ^ {2. 5} + H ^ {3}\right). \tag {C.18} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to Lemma C.6, the second inequality holds due to Lemma C.5, the last equality holds due to the fact that  $\widehat{\beta}_K = \tilde{O} (\sqrt{d}),\lambda = 1 / B^2,$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \check {\beta} _ {K} = 8 d \sqrt {\log (1 + K / \lambda) \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (d), \\ \widetilde {\beta} _ {K} = 8 \sqrt {d H ^ {4} \log (1 + K H ^ {4} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta)} + 4 H ^ {2} \log (4 k ^ {2} H / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (\sqrt {d} H ^ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, by the fact that  $x \leq a\sqrt{x} + b$  implies  $x \leq c(a^2 + b)$  with some  $c > 0$ , (C.18) yields that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} \bar {\sigma} _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq \widetilde {O} \left(d ^ {2} H ^ {5} + H ^ {2} T / d + T H + \sqrt {T} d ^ {1. 5} H ^ {2. 5}\right) \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(d ^ {2} H ^ {5} + d ^ {4} H ^ {3} + T H + H ^ {2} T / d\right), \tag {C.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the equality holds since  $\sqrt{T} d^{1.5}H^{2.5}\leq (TH^2 /d + d^4 H^3) / 2$ . Substituting (C.19) into (C.17), we have

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} \left(M _ {\Theta^ {*}}, K\right) = \widetilde {O} \left(\sqrt {d ^ {2} H ^ {2} + d H ^ {3}} \sqrt {T} + d ^ {2} H ^ {3} + d ^ {3} H ^ {2}\right),
$$

finishing the proof.

# C.3 Proof of Theorem 5.6

We select  $\delta = 1 / H$  as suggested in Section 5.3. For brevity, with a slight abuse of notation, we will use  $M_{\mu}$  to denote the MDP described in Section 5.3 corresponding to the parameters  $\pmb{\mu} = (\pmb{\mu}_1,\dots,\pmb{\mu}_H)$ . We will use  $\mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\mu}}$  denote the expectation underlying the distribution generated from the interconnection of a policy and MDP  $M_{\mu}$ ; since the policy is not denoted, we tacitly

assume that the identity of the policy will always be clear from the context. We will similarly use  $\mathbb{P}_{\mu}$  to denote the corresponding probability measure.

We start with a lemma that will be the basis of our argument that shows that the regret in our MDP can be lower bounded by the regret of  $H / 2$  bandit instances:

Lemma C.7. Suppose  $H \geq 3$  and  $3(d - 1)\Delta \leq \delta$ . Fix  $\pmb{\mu} \in (\{-\Delta, \Delta\}^{d - 1})^H$ . Fix a possibly history-dependent policy  $\pi$  and define  $\bar{a}_h^\pi = \mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\mu}}[\pmb{a}_h \mid s_h = x_h, s_1 = x_1]$ : the expected action taken by the policy when it visits state  $x_h$  in stage  $h$  provided that the initial state is  $x_1$ . Then, letting  $V^* \left( V^\pi \right)$  be the optimal value function (the value function of policy  $\pi$ , respectively), we have

$$
V _ {1} ^ {*} (x _ {1}) - V _ {1} ^ {\pi} (x _ {1}) \geq \frac {H}{1 0} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} \Big (\max  _ {\mathbf {a} \in \mathcal {A}} \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}, \mathbf {a} \rangle - \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}, \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {h} ^ {\pi} \rangle \Big).
$$

Proof. Fix  $\pmb{\mu}$ . Since  $\pmb{\mu}$  is fixed, we drop the subindex from  $\mathbb{P}$  and  $\mathbb{E}$ . Since  $\mathcal{A} = \{+1, -1\}^{d-1}$  and  $\pmb{\mu}_h \in \{-\Delta, \Delta\}^{d-1}$ , we have  $(d-1)\Delta = \max_{\mathbf{a} \in \mathcal{A}} \langle \pmb{\mu}_h, \mathbf{a} \rangle$ . Recall the definition of the value of policy  $\pi$  in state  $x_1$ :

$$
V _ {1} ^ {\pi} \left(x _ {1}\right) = \mathbb {E} \left[ \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} r _ {h} \left(s _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) \mid s _ {1} = x _ {1}, a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {h} (\cdot | s _ {1}, a _ {1}, \dots , s _ {h - 1}, a _ {h - 1}, s _ {h}) \right]. \tag {C.20}
$$

Note that by the definition of our MDPs, only  $x_{H + 2}$  satisfies that  $r_h(x_{H + 2},\mathbf{a}) = 1$ , all other rewards are zero. Also, once entered, the process does not leave  $x_{H + 2}$ . Therefore,

$$
V _ {1} ^ {\pi} \left(x _ {1}\right) = \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H - 1} (H - h) \mathbb {P} \left(N _ {h} \mid s _ {1} = x _ {1}\right). \tag {C.21}
$$

where  $N_{h}$  is the event of visiting state  $x_{h}$  in stage  $h$  and then entering  $x_{H + 2}$ :

$$
N _ {h} = \left\{s _ {h + 1} = x _ {H + 2}, s _ {h} = x _ {h} \right\}. \tag {C.22}
$$

By the law of total probability, the Markov property and the definition of  $M_{\mu}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(s _ {h + 1} = x _ {H + 2} \mid s _ {h} = x _ {h}, s _ {1} = x _ {1}\right) \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathcal {A}} \mathbb {P} (s _ {h + 1} = x _ {H + 2} | s _ {h} = x _ {h}, a _ {h} = \boldsymbol {a}) \mathbb {P} (a _ {h} = \boldsymbol {a} | s _ {h} = x _ {h}, s _ {1} = x _ {1}) \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathcal {A}} (\delta + \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}, \boldsymbol {a} \rangle) \mathbb {P} (a _ {h} = \boldsymbol {a} | s _ {h} = x _ {h}, s _ {1} = x _ {1}) \\ = \delta + \left\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}, \bar {\boldsymbol {\alpha}} _ {h} ^ {\pi} \right\rangle , \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last equality used that by definition,  $\bar{\pmb{a}}_h^\pi = \sum_{\pmb {a}\in \mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}(a_h = \pmb {a}|s_h = x_h,s_1 = x_1)\pmb {a}$ . It also follows that  $\mathbb{P}(s_{h + 1} = x_{h + 1}|s_h = x_h,s_1 = x_1) = 1 - (\delta +\langle \pmb {\mu}_h,\bar{\pmb{a}}_h^\pi \rangle)$ . Hence,

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(N _ {h}\right) = \left(\delta + \left\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}, \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {h} ^ {\pi} \right\rangle\right) \prod_ {j = 1} ^ {h - 1} \left(1 - \delta - \left\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {j}, \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {j} ^ {\pi} \right\rangle\right). \tag {C.23}
$$

Defining  $a_{h} = \langle \pmb{\mu}_{h},\bar{\pmb{a}}_{h}^{\pi}\rangle$  , we get that

$$
V _ {1} ^ {\pi} (x _ {1}) = \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} (H - h) (a _ {h} + \delta) \prod_ {j = 1} ^ {h - 1} (1 - a _ {j} - \delta).
$$

Working backwards, it is not hard to see that the optimal policy must take at stage the action that maximizes  $\langle \pmb{\mu}_h, \pmb{a} \rangle$ . Since  $\max_{a \in \mathcal{A}} \langle \pmb{\mu}_h, \pmb{a} \rangle = (d - 1)\Delta$ , we get

$$
V _ {1} ^ {*} (x _ {1}) = \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H} (H - h) (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {h - 1} ((d - 1) \Delta + \delta).
$$

For  $i\in [H]$  ,introduce

$$
S _ {i} = \sum_ {h = i} ^ {H} (H - h) \prod_ {j = i} ^ {h - 1} (1 - a _ {j} - \delta) (a _ {h} + \delta), T _ {i} = \sum_ {h = i} ^ {H} (H - h) (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {h - i} ((d - 1) \Delta + \delta).
$$

Then  $V_{1}^{*}(x_{1}) - V_{1}^{\pi}(x_{1}) = T_{1} - S_{1}$ . To lower bound  $T_{1} - S_{1}$ , first note that

$$
S _ {i} = (H - i) (a _ {i} + \delta) + S _ {i + 1} (1 - a _ {i} - \delta), T _ {i} = (H - i) ((d - 1) \Delta + \delta) + T _ {i + 1} (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta),
$$

which gives that

$$
T _ {i} - S _ {i} = (H - i - T _ {i + 1}) ((d - 1) \Delta - a _ {i}) + (1 - a _ {i} - \delta) \left(T _ {i + 1} - S _ {i + 1}\right). \tag {C.24}
$$

Therefore by induction, we get that

$$
T _ {1} - S _ {1} = \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H - 1} ((d - 1) \Delta - a _ {h}) (H - h - T _ {h + 1}) \prod_ {j = 1} ^ {h - 1} (1 - a _ {j} - \delta). \tag {C.25}
$$

To further bound (C.25), first we note that  $T_{h}$  can be written as the following closed-form expression:

$$
T _ {h} = \frac {(1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {H - h} - 1}{(d - 1) \Delta + \delta} + H - h + 1 - (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {H - h},
$$

Hence, for any  $h \leq H / 2$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} H - h - T _ {h + 1} = \frac {1 - (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {H - h}}{(d - 1) \Delta + \delta} + (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {H - h} \\ \geq \frac {1 - (1 - (d - 1) \Delta - \delta) ^ {H / 2}}{(d - 1) \Delta + \delta} \geq H / 3, \tag {C.26} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality holds since  $3(d - 1)\Delta \leq \delta = 1 / H$  and  $H\geq 3$ . Furthermore we have

$$
\prod_ {j = 1} ^ {h - 1} \left(1 - a _ {j} - \delta\right) \geq \left(1 - 4 \delta / 3\right) ^ {H} \geq 1 / 3, \tag {C.27}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $a_{j} \leq (d - 1)\Delta, 3(d - 1)\Delta \leq \delta$ , the second one holds since  $\delta = 1 / H$  and  $H \geq 3$ . Therefore, substituting (C.26) and (C.27) into (C.25), we have

$$
V _ {1} ^ {*} (x _ {1}) - V _ {1} ^ {\pi} (x _ {1}) = T _ {1} - S _ {1} \geq \frac {H}{1 0} \cdot \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} ((d - 1) \Delta - a _ {h}),
$$

which finishes the proof.

We also need a lower bound on the regret on linear bandits with the hypercube action set  $\mathcal{A} = \{-1,1\}^{d - 1}$ , Bernoulli bandits with linear mean payoff. While the proof technique used is standard (cf. Lattimore and Szepesvári 2020), we give the full proof as the "scaling" of the reward parameters is nonstandard:

Lemma C.8. Fix a positive real  $0 < \delta \leq 1/3$ , and positive integers  $K, d$  and assume that  $K \geq d^2/(2\delta)$ . Let  $\Delta = \sqrt{\delta / K} / (4\sqrt{2})$  and consider the linear bandit problems  $\mathcal{L}_{\mu}$  parameterized with a parameter vector  $\pmb{\mu} \in \{-\Delta, \Delta\}^d$  and action set  $\mathcal{A} = \{-1, 1\}^d$  so that the reward distribution for taking action  $\pmb{a} \in \mathcal{A}$  is a Bernoulli distribution  $B(\delta + \langle \pmb{\mu}^*, \mathbf{a} \rangle)$ . Then for any bandit algorithm  $\mathcal{B}$ , there exists a  $\pmb{\mu}^* \in \{-\Delta, \Delta\}^d$  such that the expected pseudo-regret of  $\mathcal{B}$  over first  $K$  steps on bandit  $\mathcal{L}_{\mu^*}$  is lower bounded as follows:

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {*}} \operatorname {R e g r e t} (K) \geq \frac {d \sqrt {K \delta}}{8 \sqrt {2}}.
$$

Note that the expectation is with respect to a distribution that depends both on  $\mathcal{B}$  and  $\pmb{\mu}^{*}$ , but since  $\mathcal{B}$  is fixed, this dependence is hidden.

Proof. Let  $\mathbf{a}_k \in \mathcal{A} = \{-1, 1\}^d$  denote the action chosen in round  $k$ . Then for any  $\pmb{\mu} \in \{-\Delta, \Delta\}^d$ , the expected pseudo regret  $\mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\mu}} \mathrm{Regret}(K)$  corresponding to  $\pmb{\mu}$  satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \mathrm {R e g r e t} (K) = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} (\max _ {\mathbf {a} \in \mathcal {A}} \langle \boldsymbol {\mu}, \mathbf {a} \rangle - \langle \boldsymbol {\mu}, \mathbf {a} _ {k} \rangle) = \Delta \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \mathbb {1} \left\{\operatorname {s g n} ([ \boldsymbol {\mu} ] _ {j}) \neq \operatorname {s g n} ([ \mathbf {a} _ {k} ] _ {j}) \right\} \\ = \Delta \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \underbrace {\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \mathbb {1} \left\{\operatorname {s g n} \left(\left[ \boldsymbol {\mu} \right] _ {j}\right) \neq \operatorname {s g n} \left(\left[ \mathbf {a} _ {k} \right] _ {j}\right) \right\}} _ {N _ {j} (\boldsymbol {\mu})}, \tag {C.28} \\ \end{array}
$$

where for a vector  $\pmb{x}$ , we use  $[\pmb{x}]_j$  to denote its  $j$ th entry. Let  $\pmb{\mu}^j \in \{-\Delta, \Delta\}^d$  denote the vector which differs from  $\pmb{\mu}$  at its  $j$ th coordinate only. Then, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 2 \sum_ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \operatorname {R e g r e t} (K) = \Delta \sum_ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \left(\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} N _ {j} (\boldsymbol {\mu}) + \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j}} N _ {j} \left(\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j}\right)\right) \\ = \Delta \sum_ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} (K + \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} N _ {j} (\boldsymbol {\mu}) - \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j}} N _ {j} (\boldsymbol {\mu})) \\ \geq \Delta \sum_ {\mu} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \left(K - \sqrt {1 / 2} K \sqrt {\operatorname {K L} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\mu} , \mathcal {P} _ {\mu^ {j}}\right)}\right), \tag {C.29} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the inequality holds due to  $N_{j}(\pmb{\mu}) \in [0, K]$  and Pinsker's inequality (Exercise 14.4 and Eq. 14.12, Lattimore and Szepesvári 2020),  $\mathcal{P}_{\pmb{\mu}}$  denotes the joint distribution over the all possible reward sequences  $(r_1, \ldots, r_K) \in \{0, 1\}^K$  of length  $K$ , induced by the interconnection of the algorithm and the bandit parameterized by  $\pmb{\mu}$ . By the chain rule of relative entropy,  $\mathrm{KL}(\mathcal{P}_{\pmb{\mu}}, \mathcal{P}_{\pmb{\mu}^j})$  can be further decomposed as (cf. Exercise 14.11 of Lattimore and Szepesvári 2020),

$$
\operatorname {K L} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}}, \mathcal {P} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j}}\right) = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \left[ \operatorname {K L} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \left(r _ {k} \mid \mathbf {r} _ {1: k - 1}\right), \mathcal {P} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j}} \left(r _ {k} \mid \mathbf {r} _ {1: k - 1}\right)\right) \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} [ \mathrm {K L} (B (\delta + \langle \mathbf {a} _ {k}, \boldsymbol {\mu} \rangle), (B (\delta + \langle \mathbf {a} _ {k}, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j} \rangle))) ] \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \left[ \frac {2 \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} - \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {j} , \mathbf {a} _ {k} \rangle^ {2}}{\langle \boldsymbol {\mu} , \mathbf {a} _ {k} \rangle + \delta} \right] \\ \leq \frac {1 6 K \Delta^ {2}}{\delta}, \tag {C.30} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second equality holds since the round  $k$  reward's distribution is the Bernoulli distribution  $B(\delta + \langle \mathbf{a}_k, \boldsymbol{\mu} \rangle)$  in the environment parameterized by  $\boldsymbol{\mu}$ , the first inequality holds since for any two Bernoulli distribution  $B(a)$  and  $B(b)$ , we have  $\mathrm{KL}(B(a), B(b)) \leq 2(a - b)^2 / a$  when  $a \leq 1/2$ ,  $a + b \leq 1$ , the second inequality holds since  $\boldsymbol{\mu}$  only differs from  $\boldsymbol{\mu}^j$  at  $j$ -th coordinate,  $\langle \boldsymbol{\mu}, \mathbf{a}_k \rangle \geq -d\Delta \geq -\delta / 2$ . It can be verified that these requirements hold when  $\delta \leq 1/3$ ,  $d\Delta \leq \delta / 2$ . Therefore, substituting (C.30) into (C.29), we have

$$
2 \sum_ {\mu} \mathbb {E} _ {\mu} \mathrm {R e g r e t} (K) \geq \sum_ {\mu} \Delta d (K - \sqrt {2} K ^ {3 / 2} \Delta / \sqrt {\delta}) = \sum_ {\mu} \frac {d \sqrt {K \delta}}{4 \sqrt {2}},
$$

where the equality holds since  $\Delta = \sqrt{\delta / K} /(4\sqrt{2})$ . Selecting  $\pmb{\mu}^{*}$  which maximizes  $\mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\mu}}\mathrm{Regret}(K)$  finishes the proof.

With this, we are ready to prove Theorem 5.6.

Proof of Theorem 5.6. We can verify that the selection of  $K, d, H, \delta$  satisfy the requirement of Lemma C.7 and Lemma C.8. Let  $\pi^k$  denote the possibly nonstationary policy that is executed in episode  $k$  given the history up to the beginning of the episode. Then, by Lemma C.7, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \operatorname {R e g r e t} \Big (M _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}}, K \Big) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \Big [ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left[ V _ {1} ^ {*} \left(x _ {1}\right) - V _ {1} ^ {\pi^ {k}} \left(x _ {1}\right) \right] \Big ] \\ \geq \frac {H}{1 0} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} \underbrace {\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \left[ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {K} \left(\max  _ {\mathbf {a} \in \mathcal {A}} \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h} , \mathbf {a} \rangle - \langle \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h} , \bar {\boldsymbol {a}} _ {h} ^ {\pi_ {k}} \rangle\right) \right]} _ {I _ {h} (\boldsymbol {\mu}, \pi)}. \tag {C.31} \\ \end{array}
$$

Let  $\pmb{\mu}^{-h} = (\pmb{\mu}_1, \dots, \pmb{\mu}_{h-1}, \pmb{\mu}_{h+1}, \dots, \pmb{\mu}_H)$ . Now, every MDP policy  $\pi$  gives rise to a bandit algorithm  $\mathcal{B}_{\pi, h, \pmb{\mu}^{-h}}$  for the linear bandit  $\mathcal{L}_{\pmb{\mu}_h}$  of Lemma C.8. This bandit algorithm is such that the distribution of action it plays in round  $k$  matches the distribution of action played by  $\pi$  in stage  $h$  of episode  $k$  conditioned on the event that  $s_h^k = x_h$ , i.e.,  $\mathbb{P}_{\mu, \pi}(a_h^k = \cdot | s_h^k = x_h)$  with the tacit assumption that the first state in every episode is  $x_1$ .

As the notation suggests, the bandit algorithm depends on  $\pmb{\mu}^{-h}$ . In particular, to play in round  $k$ , the bandit algorithm feeds  $\pi$  with data from the MDP kernels up until the beginning of episode  $k$ : For  $i \neq h$ , this can be done by just following  $\mathbb{P}_i$  since the parameters of these kernels is known to  $\mathcal{B}_{\pi,h,\pmb{\mu}^{-h}}$ . When  $i = h$ , since  $\mathbb{P}_h$  is not available to the bandit algorithm, every time it is on stage  $h$ , if the state is  $x_h$ , it feeds the action obtained from  $\pi$  to  $\mathcal{L}_{\mu}$  and if the reward is 1, it feeds  $\pi$  with the next state  $x_{H+2}$ , otherwise it feeds it with next state  $x_{h+1}$ . When  $i = h$  and the state is not  $x_h$ , it can only be  $x_{H+2}$ , in which case the next state fed to  $\pi$  is  $x_{H+2}$  regardless of the action it takes. At

the beginning of episode  $k$ , to ensure that state  $x_{h}$  is "reached",  $\pi$  is fed with the states  $x_{1}, x_{2}, \ldots, x_{h}$ . Then,  $\pi$  is queried for its action, which is the action that the bandit plays in round  $k$ . Clearly, by this construction, the distribution of action played in round  $k$  by  $\mathcal{B}_{\pi,h,\mu^{-h}}$  matches the target.

Denoting by BanditRegret  $(\mathcal{B}_{\pi,h,\boldsymbol{\mu}^{-h}},\boldsymbol{\mu}_h)$  the regret of this bandit algorithm on  $\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}$ , by our construction,

$$
I _ {h} (\boldsymbol {\mu}, \pi) = \operatorname {B a n d i t R e g r e t} \left(\mathcal {B} _ {\pi , h, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {- h}}, \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}\right)
$$

for all  $h\in [H / 2]$  . Hence,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \operatorname {R e g r e t} \left(M _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}}, K\right) \geq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {H}{1 0} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} \operatorname {B a n d i t R e g r e t} \left(\mathcal {B} _ {\pi , h, \boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {- h}}, \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}\right) \\ \geq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\mu}} \frac {H}{1 0} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} \inf  _ {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {- h}} \operatorname {B a n d i t R e g r e t} \left(\mathcal {B} _ {\pi , h, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {- h}}, \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}\right) \\ = \frac {H}{1 0} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {H / 2} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\mu} ^ {h}} \inf  _ {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {- h}} \operatorname {B a n d i t R e g r e t} \left(\mathcal {B} _ {\pi , h, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mu}} ^ {- h}}, \boldsymbol {\mu} _ {h}\right) \\ \geq \frac {H ^ {2}}{2 0} \frac {(d - 1) \sqrt {K \delta}}{8 \sqrt {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows by Lemma C.8. The result follows by plugging in  $\delta = 1 / H$  and  $T = KH$ .

# D Proof of Main Results in Section 6

Here we provide the proof for the results in Section 6. For this, let  $\pi$  denote the policy implemented by  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$ . Note that this is a slight abuse of notation since  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  already defines  $\pi_t$ . However, the two definitions are consistent:  $\pi_t$  as defined in  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  depends on the history, which is only made explicit by  $\pi$ .

Further, by a slight abuse of notation, we let  $\mathbb{P}$  be the distribution over  $(\mathcal{S} \times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$  induced by the interconnection of  $\pi$  and the MDP  $M$ . Further, let  $\mathbb{E}$  be the corresponding expectation operator. Note that the only source of randomness are the stochastic transitions in the MDP, hence, all random variables can be defined over the sample space  $\Omega = (\mathcal{S} \times \mathcal{A})^{\mathbb{N}}$ . Thus, we work with the probability space given by the triplet  $(\Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P})$ , where  $\mathcal{F}$  is the product  $\sigma$ -algebra generated by the discrete  $\sigma$ -algebras underlying  $\mathcal{S}$  and  $\mathcal{A}$ , respectively.

Let  $\mathcal{F}_t = \sigma(s_1, \ldots, s_t)$  denote the  $\sigma$ -algebra generated by  $s_1, \ldots, s_t$ . Then  $Q_t, V_t, \pi_t, \widehat{\Sigma}_t, \widetilde{\Sigma}_t$  are  $\mathcal{F}_{t-1}$ -measurable and  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \bar{\sigma}_t, E_t, \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_t$  are  $\mathcal{F}_t$ -measurable.

# D.1 Proof of Lemma 6.1

Let  $\tilde{\mathcal{C}}_t, \tilde{\mathcal{C}}_t$  denote the following confidence sets:

$$
\check {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} = \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta}: \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \check {\beta} _ {t} \right\},
$$

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} = \left\{\boldsymbol {\theta}: \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {\theta} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \widetilde {\beta} _ {t} \right\}.
$$

Then similar to the proof of Lemma 5.2, here we prove a stronger statement: with probability at least  $1 - 3\delta$ , simultaneously for all  $1 \leq t \leq T$ , we have

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \in \widehat {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} \cap \check {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} \cap \widetilde {\mathcal {C}} _ {t} \cap \mathcal {B}, | [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) | \leq E _ {t}.
$$

We need the following lemma.

Lemma D.1. Let  $V_{t},\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{t},\widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{t},\widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{t},\widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{t}$  be as defined in Algorithm 3, then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) - \left[ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right| \\ \leq \min  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2}, \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \tilde {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ + \min  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2}, 2 \bar {H} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. The proof is the same as that of Lemma C.1 with  $H$  replaced by  $\bar{H}$ ,  $V_{k,h+1}$  replaced by  $V_t$ ,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h}, \widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h}$  replaced by  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_t, \widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_t$ , respectively, and  $\pmb{\theta}_h^*$  replaced by  $\pmb{\theta}^*$ .

Proof of Lemma 6.1. The proof is the same as that of Lemma 5.2 with Lemma C.1 replaced by Lemma D.1,  $H$  replaced by  $\bar{H}$ ,  $V_{k,h+1}$  replaced by  $V_t$ ,  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{k,h}, \widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h}, \widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_{k,h}$  replaced by  $\widehat{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \widetilde{\pmb{\theta}}_t, \widehat{\pmb{\Sigma}}_t, \widetilde{\pmb{\Sigma}}_t$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}_h^*$  replaced by  $\pmb{\theta}^*$ . Note that the definitions of  $\widehat{\beta}_t, \widehat{\beta}_t, \widetilde{\beta}_t$  slightly differ from those of  $\widehat{\beta}_{k,h}, \widehat{\beta}_{k,h}, \widetilde{\beta}_{k,h}$  since we do not need to take a union bound over  $\bar{H}$  in the discounted setting. Finally, using the fact that  $\pmb{\theta}^* \in \mathcal{B}$  yields our result.

# D.2 Proof of Theorem 6.3

In this section we prove Theorem 6.3.

Let  $K(T) - 1$  be the number of epochs (counter  $k$ ) when  $\mathrm{UCLK}^+$  finishes after  $T$  rounds. For convenience, we also set  $t_{K(T)} = T + 1$ . Let  $\mathcal{E}$  denote the event when the conclusion of Lemma 6.1 holds. Then by Lemma 6.1 we have  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}) \geq 1 - 3\delta$ . Define the events  $\mathcal{E}_1$  and  $\mathcal{E}_2$  as follows:

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {1} = \Bigg \{\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \Big \{\big [ \mathbb {P} (V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}) \big ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - \big (V _ {t} (s _ {t + 1}) - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} (s _ {t + 1}) \big) \Big \} \leq 4 \bar {H} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)} \Bigg \},
$$

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {2} = \left\{\gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \leq 5 \bar {H} T + 2 5 / 3 \cdot \bar {H} ^ {3} \sqrt {\log (\bar {H} / \delta)} \right\}.
$$

Then we have  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_1) \geq 1 - \delta$  and  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_2) \geq 1 - \delta$ . The first one holds since  $M_t \coloneqq [\mathbb{P}(V_t - V_{t+1}^\pi)](s_t, a_t) - (V_t(s_{t+1}) - V_{t+1}^\pi(s_{t+1}))$  forms a martingale difference sequence, and we have  $|M_t| \leq 4H$ . Indeed, since  $\pi_{t+1}$  is  $\mathcal{F}_t$ -measurable, so is  $V_{t+1}^\pi(\cdot) = Q_{t+1}^\pi(\cdot, \pi_{t+1}(\cdot))$ . As a result, and thanks to also  $a_t$  and  $V_t$  being  $\mathcal{F}_t$ -measurable,  $\mathbb{E}[M_t | \mathcal{F}_t] = 0$ . By the above measurability observations and because  $s_{t+1}$  is by definition  $\mathcal{F}_{t+1}$ -measurable, it follows that  $M_t$  is  $\mathcal{F}_{t+1}$ -measurable, hence  $\mathcal{F}_t$ -adapted. Then Lemma C.2 implies that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\{\left[ \mathbb {P} (V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}) \right] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - \left(V _ {t} (s _ {t + 1}) - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} (s _ {t + 1})\right) \right\} \leq 4 \bar {H} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)},
$$

which gives  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_1) \geq 1 - \delta$ . That  $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_2) \geq 1 - \delta$  holds follows from the following lemma:

Lemma D.2 (Total variance bound, Lemma A.6, He et al. 2020b). With probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , we have

$$
\gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \leq 5 \bar {H} T + 2 5 / 3 \cdot \bar {H} ^ {3} \sqrt {\log (\bar {H} / \delta)}.
$$

Based on  $\mathcal{E}$ ,  $\mathcal{E}_1$  and  $\mathcal{E}_2$ , we start with a number of technical lemmas.

Lemma D.3 (Lemma 6.2, Zhou et al. 2020). On the event  $\mathcal{E}$ , for any  $(s,a)\in S\times \mathcal{A}$  and  $1\leq t\leq T$ ,  $\bar{H}\ge Q_t(s,a)\ge Q^* (s,a)$ , and  $\bar{H}\ge V_{t}(s)\ge V^{*}(s)$  hold.

Lemma D.4 (Lemma 6.3, Zhou et al. 2020). On the event  $\mathcal{E}$ , for any  $0 \leq k \leq K(T) - 1$  and  $t_k \leq t \leq t_{k+1} - 1$ , there exists  $\pmb{\theta}_t \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{t_k} \cap \mathcal{B}$  such that  $Q_t(s_t, a_t) \leq r(s_t, a_t) + \gamma \langle \pmb{\theta}_t, \phi_{V_t}(s_t, a_t) \rangle + 2\gamma^U$ .

Lemma D.5. Let  $K(T)$  be as defined above. Then,  $K(T) \leq 2d\log(1 + Td / \lambda)$ .

Proof. For simplicity, we denote  $K = K(T)$ . Note that  $\operatorname{det}(\Sigma_0) = \lambda^d$ . We further have

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {T} \| _ {2} = \left\| \lambda \mathbf {I} + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) ^ {\top} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} \right\| _ {2} \leq \lambda + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\| \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} \leq \lambda + d T, \quad (\mathrm {D}. 1)
$$

where the first inequality holds due to the triangle inequality, the second inequality holds due to the fact  $V_{t}(\cdot) \leq \bar{H}$ ,  $\bar{\sigma}_{t}^{2} \geq \bar{H}^{2} / d$  and  $\left\| \phi_{V_t}(s_t,a_t)\right\| _2 \leq \bar{H}$ . Inequality (D.1) implies that  $\operatorname*{det}(\pmb{\Sigma}_T) \leq (\lambda +dT)^d$ . Therefore, we have

$$
\left(\lambda + d T\right) ^ {d} \geq \det  \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {T}\right) \geq \det  \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t _ {K - 1}}\right) \geq 2 ^ {K - 1} \det  \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t _ {0}}\right) = 2 ^ {K - 1} \lambda^ {d}, \tag {D.2}
$$

where the second inequality holds since  $\pmb{\Sigma}_{T} \succeq \pmb{\Sigma}_{t_{K-1}}$ , the third inequality holds due to the fact that  $\operatorname{det}(\pmb{\Sigma}_{t_k}) \geq 2 \operatorname{det}(\pmb{\Sigma}_{t_{k-1}})$  by the update rule in Algorithm 3. Inequality (D.2) implies that

$$
K \leq d \log (1 + d T / \lambda) + 1 \leq 2 d \log (1 + d T / \lambda),
$$

which ends our proof.

Lemma D.6. Let  $V_{t}$  be as defined in Algorithm 3. Then, on the event  $\mathcal{E}_1$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \leq 4 \bar {H} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)} + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t}\right) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right)\right) + 4 \bar {H} K (T).
$$

Proof. Assume that  $\mathcal{E}_1$  holds. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \\ = \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\{\left[ \mathbb {P} \left(V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}\right) \right] \left(s _ {t} , a _ {t}\right) - \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right)\right) \right\}} _ {I _ {1}} + \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right)\right)} _ {I _ {2}}. \tag {D.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

For the term  $I_{1}$ , by the definition of  $\mathcal{E}_1$ , we have

$$
I _ {1} \leq 4 \bar {H} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)}. \tag {D.4}
$$

For the term  $I_{2}$ , first note that the value function  $V_{t}$  does not change over the intervals  $[t_{1}, t_{2} - 1]$ ,  $[t_{2}, t_{3} - 1]$ , ..., Hence,

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {2} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} V _ {t} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) \\ = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 2} V _ {t} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) + V _ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} \left(s _ {t _ {k + 1}}\right) \right] - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) \\ = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 2} V _ {t + 1} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) + V _ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} \left(s _ {t _ {k + 1}}\right) \right] - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) \\ \leq \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} V _ {t} \left(s _ {t}\right) + V _ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} \left(s _ {t _ {k + 1}}\right) \right] - \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t + 1}\right) \\ = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} (s _ {t}) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} (s _ {t})\right) + V _ {1} ^ {\pi} (s _ {1}) - V _ {T + 1} ^ {\pi} (s _ {T + 1}) + \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} V _ {t _ {k + 1}} (s _ {t _ {k + 1} + 1}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third equality holds since  $V_{t}(\cdot) = V_{t + 1}(\cdot)$  for any  $t_k \leq t \leq t_{k + 1} - 2$ . Using the fact that  $0 \leq V_t(\cdot), V_t^\pi (\cdot) \leq \bar{H}$ ,  $I_2$  can be further bounded as

$$
I _ {2} \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t}\right) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right)\right) + \bar {H} + \bar {H} K (T) \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t}\right) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right)\right) + 2 \bar {H} K (T). \tag {D.5}
$$

Substituting (D.4) and (D.5) into (D.3) gets our result.

Lemma D.7. Let  $V_{t}, \bar{\sigma}_{t}, \widetilde{\beta}_{T}, \check{\beta}_{T}$  be as defined in Algorithm 3. Then, on the event  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_2$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} \leq \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {2} T / d + 2 \gamma^ {2} \widetilde {\beta} _ {T} \sqrt {2 d T \log (1 + T \bar {H} ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {1. 5} \breve {\beta} _ {T} \sqrt {2 d T \log (1 + T / \lambda)} \\ + 5 \bar {H} T + 2 5 / 3 \cdot \bar {H} ^ {3} \sqrt {\log (\bar {H} / \delta)} + 2 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. Assume that  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}'$  holds. Then, by Lemma D.3,  $\bar{H} \geq Q_t(\cdot, \cdot) \geq Q^*(\cdot, \cdot)$ ,  $\bar{H} \geq V_t(\cdot) \geq V^*(\cdot)$  holds for any  $1 \leq t \leq T$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} = \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \max  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2} / d, [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) + E _ {t} \right\} \\ = \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \max  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2} / d, [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) + 2 E _ {t} + [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - E _ {t} \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {2} T / d + \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) + 2 E _ {t} \right] + \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - E _ {t} \right] \\ \leq \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {2} T / d + \underbrace {2 \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} E _ {t}} _ {I _ {1}} + \underbrace {\gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} ] \left(s _ {t} , a _ {t}\right)} _ {I _ {2}} + \underbrace {\gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] \left(s _ {t} , a _ {t}\right) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} ] \left(s _ {t} , a _ {t}\right) \right]} _ {I _ {3}}, \tag {D.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality is by the definition of  $\mathcal{E}$ . To bound  $I_{1}$ , recall that by the definition of  $E_{t}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {1} \leq 2 \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \widetilde {\beta} _ {t} \min \left\{1, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \right\} + 2 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \check {\beta} _ {t} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \min \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} \right\} \\ \leq 2 \gamma^ {2} \widetilde {\beta} _ {T} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \right\} + 7 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {2} \check {\beta} _ {T} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} \right\}, \tag {D.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since  $\widetilde{\beta}_t \geq \bar{H}^2$ ,  $\check{\beta}_t\bar{\sigma}_t \geq \bar{H}$ , the second inequality holds since  $\widetilde{\beta}_T \geq \widetilde{\beta}_t$  and  $\bar{\sigma}_t \leq \sqrt{3}\bar{H}$ , since by the definition of  $\bar{\sigma}_t$ ,

$$
\bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} = \max  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2} / d, [ \bar {\mathbb {V}} _ {t} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) + E _ {t} \right\} \leq \max  \left\{\bar {H} ^ {2} / d, \bar {H} ^ {2} + 2 \bar {H} ^ {2} \right\} = 3 \bar {H} ^ {2}.
$$

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min \left\{1, \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} \right\} \leq \sqrt {T} \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min \left\{1 , \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t} ^ {2}} (s _ {t} , a _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq \sqrt {2 d T \log \left(1 + T \bar {H} ^ {4} / (d \lambda)\right)}, \tag {D.8} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds due to Lemma B.2. Similarly, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1, \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} \right\} \leq \sqrt {T} \sqrt {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1 , \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t} , a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \right\}} \\ \leq \sqrt {2 d T \log (1 + T / \lambda)}. \tag {D.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

Substituting (D.8) and (D.9) into (D.7), we get

$$
I _ {1} \leq 2 \gamma^ {2} \widetilde {\beta} _ {T} \sqrt {2 d T \log (1 + T \bar {H} ^ {4} / (d \lambda))} + 7 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {2} \check {\beta} _ {T} \sqrt {2 d T \log (1 + T / \lambda)}. \tag {D.10}
$$

To bound  $I_{2}$ , since  $\mathcal{E}_2$  holds, then we have

$$
I _ {2} \leq 5 \bar {H} T + 2 5 / 3 \cdot \bar {H} ^ {3} \sqrt {\log (\bar {H} / \delta)}. \tag {D.11}
$$

To bound  $I_{3}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - [ \mathbb {V} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} ] (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right] \\ = \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} V _ {t} ^ {2} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) - \left[ \mathbb {P} \left(V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}\right) ^ {2} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) - \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} V _ {t} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right] ^ {2} + \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right] ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} ^ {2} - \left[ V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] ^ {2} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \\ \leq 2 \gamma^ {2} \bar {H} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right), \tag {D.12} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds since on  $\mathcal{E}$ , by Lemma D.3,  $0 \leq V_{t+1}^{\pi}(\cdot) \leq V^{*}(\cdot) \leq V_{t}(\cdot)$ , the second inequality holds since  $0 \leq V_{t+1}^{\pi}(\cdot), V_{t}(\cdot) \leq \bar{H}$ . Substituting (D.10), (D.11) and (D.12) into (D.6) ends our proof.

Lemma D.8 (Lemma 12, Abbasi-Yadkori et al. 2011). Suppose  $\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\in \mathbb{R}^{d\times d}$  are two positive definite matrices satisfying that  $\mathbf{A}\succeq \mathbf{B}$ , then for any  $\mathbf{x}\in \mathbb{R}^d$ $\| \mathbf{x}\|_{\mathbf{A}}\leq \| \mathbf{x}\|_{\mathbf{B}}\cdot \sqrt{\operatorname*{det}(\mathbf{A}) / \operatorname*{det}(\mathbf{B})}$ .

With all above lemmas, we begin to prove Theorem 6.3.

Proof of Theorem 6.3. Taking a union bound, we have that with probability at least  $1 - 5\delta$ ,  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_1 \cap \mathcal{E}_2$  holds. Assume that  $\mathcal{E} \cap \mathcal{E}_1 \cap \mathcal{E}_2$  holds. We first bound  $\mathrm{Regret}'(T)$ , which is defined as follows:

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T) = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ Q _ {t} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right) \right]. \tag {D.13}
$$

Since  $\mathcal{E}$  holds, by Lemma D.4 it holds that for any  $t\leq T$  , for the unique index  $k(t)\coloneqq k\geq 0$  such that  $t_k + 1\leq t\leq t_{k + 1}$  there exists  $\pmb {\theta}_t\in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{t_k}\cap \mathcal{B}$  such that

$$
Q _ {t} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \leq r \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) + \gamma \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle + 2 \gamma^ {U}, \tag {D.14}
$$

By the definition of  $V_{t}^{\pi}$  and  $V_{t + 1}^{\pi}$  and the fact that  $a_{t} = \pi_{t}(s_{t})$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right) = r \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) + \gamma \left[ \mathbb {P} V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \\ = r \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) + \gamma \sum_ {s ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {S}} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi \left(s ^ {\prime} \mid s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \left(s ^ {\prime}\right) \\ = r \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) + \gamma \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle , \tag {D.15} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second equality holds due to Assumption 3.1. Substituting (D.14) and (D.15) into (D.13), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T) - 2 T \gamma^ {U} \\ \leq \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\rangle - \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\rangle\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \underbrace {\gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} , \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t} , a _ {t}) \right\rangle} _ {I _ {1}} + \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) - \phi_ {V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\rangle \\ = I _ {1} + \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left[ \mathbb {P} \left(V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi}\right) \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \\ \leq I _ {1} + 4 \gamma \bar {H} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)} + \gamma \underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(V _ {t} \left(s _ {t}\right) - V _ {t} ^ {\pi} \left(s _ {t}\right)\right)} _ {\text {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T)} + 2 \gamma \bar {H} K (T), \tag {D.16} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality holds due to Lemma D.6. Solving (D.16) for  $\mathrm{Regret}'(T)$  gives

$$
\operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T) \leq \bar {H} I _ {1} + 4 \gamma \bar {H} ^ {2} \sqrt {2 T \log (1 / \delta)} + 2 \gamma \bar {H} ^ {2} K (T) + 2 \bar {H} T \gamma^ {U}. \tag {D.17}
$$

Next we bound  $I_{1}$ . Take any  $1 \leq t \leq T$ . To bound the  $t$ th term of  $I_{1}$  note that by definition  $\pmb{\theta}_{t} \in \mathcal{B}$ . Hence,  $\langle \phi(s'|s_t, a_t), \pmb{\theta}_t \rangle$  is a probability distribution and thus

$$
\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle = \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle - [ \mathbb {P} V _ {t} ] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \leq \bar {H}. \tag {D.18}
$$

Meanwhile,  $I_{1}$  can be bounded in another way:

$$
\begin{array}{l} I _ {1} = \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \right\rangle \\ = \gamma \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*}, \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right\rangle \right] \\ \leq \gamma \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t _ {k}} \right\| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t}} + \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t _ {k}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t}}\right) \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \right] \\ \leq \gamma \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {K (T) - 1} \left[ \sum_ {t = t _ {k}} ^ {t _ {k + 1} - 1} 2 \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t _ {k}} \right\| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t _ {k}}} + \left\| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {t _ {k}} - \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t _ {k}}}\right) \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) \| _ {\widehat {\boldsymbol {\Sigma}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \right] \\ \leq 4 \gamma \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \widehat {\beta} _ {t _ {k}} \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\widehat {\mathbf {S}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \bar {\sigma} _ {t}, \tag {D.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality holds due to the Cauchy-Schwarz and the triangle inequalities, the second inequality holds due to Lemma D.8 and that, by construction,  $\operatorname*{det}(\pmb{\Sigma}_t) \leq 2\operatorname*{det}(\pmb{\Sigma}_{t_k})$  for  $t_k \leq t < t_{k+1}$ , while the last holds by the definition  $\mathcal{C}_{t_k}$  and that for  $k = k(t)$ , by its definition,  $\pmb{\theta}_t \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{t_k}$  and since on event  $\mathcal{E}$ ,  $\pmb{\theta}^* \in \widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{t_k}$  also holds.

Combining (D.18) and (D.19),  $I_{1}$  can be further bounded as

$$
I _ {1} \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{\bar {H}, 4 \gamma \widehat {\beta} _ {t _ {k}} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} (s _ {t}, a _ {t}) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\widehat {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \right\}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \left(4 \widehat {\beta} _ {t _ {k}} \gamma \bar {\sigma} _ {t} + \bar {H}\right) \min  \left\{1, \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\widehat {\mathbf {S}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} \right\} \\ \leq \sqrt {\underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} (4 \widehat {\beta} _ {T} \gamma \bar {\sigma} _ {t} + \bar {H}) ^ {2}} _ {J _ {1}}} \sqrt {\underbrace {\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \min  \left\{1 , \| \phi_ {V _ {t}} \left(s _ {t} , a _ {t}\right) / \bar {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\widehat {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right\}} _ {J _ {2}}}, \tag {D.20} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality holds since  $\min\{ac, bd\} \leq (a + b)\min\{c, d\}$  for  $a, b, c, d > 0$ , the last inequality holds due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and  $\widehat{\beta}_t \leq \widehat{\beta}_T$ . To further bound (D.20), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} J _ {1} \leq 2 \bar {H} ^ {2} T + 3 2 \widehat {\beta} _ {T} ^ {2} \gamma^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \bar {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {2} \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} T + d \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} T / d + d ^ {1. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} \sqrt {T} + \bar {H} T + \bar {H} ^ {3} + \bar {H} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right)\right)\right) \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(T \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H}\right) + \sqrt {T} d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H} ^ {3} + d \bar {H} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T} \mathbb {P} \left[ V _ {t} - V _ {t + 1} ^ {\pi} \right] \left(s _ {t}, a _ {t}\right)\right), \tag {D.21} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first equality holds due to Lemma D.7 and the facts

$$
\widehat {\beta_ {t}} = 8 \sqrt {d \log (1 + t / \lambda) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (\sqrt {d}),
$$

$$
\check {\beta} _ {t} = 8 d \sqrt {\log (1 + t \bar {H} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \sqrt {d} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (d),
$$

$$
\widetilde {\beta} _ {t} = 8 \sqrt {d \bar {H} ^ {4} \log (1 + t \bar {H} ^ {4} / (d \lambda)) \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta)} + 4 \bar {H} ^ {2} \log (4 t ^ {2} / \delta) + \sqrt {\lambda} B = \widetilde {\Theta} (\sqrt {d} \bar {H} ^ {2}).
$$

with the selection  $\lambda = 1 / B^2$ . Since  $\mathcal{E}_1$  holds by assumption, by Lemma D.6 we can bound  $\sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathbb{P}\big[V_t - V_{t+1}^\pi\big](s_t, a_t)$ , which leads to

$$
\begin{array}{l} J _ {1} \leq \widetilde {O} \left(T \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H}\right) + \sqrt {T} d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H} ^ {3} + d \bar {H} ^ {2} \sqrt {T} + d \bar {H} \operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T) + d \bar {H} ^ {2} K (T)\right) \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(T \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H}\right) + \sqrt {T} d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H} ^ {3} + d \bar {H} \operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T)\right) \\ = \widetilde {O} \left(T \left(\bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H}\right) + d ^ {5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d \bar {H} ^ {3} + d \bar {H} \operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T)\right), \tag {D.22} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first equality holds since  $K(T) = \widetilde{O}(d)$  by Lemma D.5, the second equality holds since  $2\sqrt{T}d^{2.5}\bar{H}^2 \leq d^5\bar{H}^2 + T\bar{H}^2$ . To bound  $J_2$ , taking  $\mathbf{x}_t = \phi_{V_t}(s_t, a_t) / \bar{\sigma}_t$  with the fact  $\| \phi_{V_t}(s_t, a_t) / \bar{\sigma}_t \|_2 \leq \bar{H} / \bar{\sigma}_t \leq \sqrt{d}$ , by Lemma B.2, we have

$$
J _ {2} \leq 2 d \log (1 + T / \lambda). \tag {D.23}
$$

Substituting (D.22) and (D.23) into (D.20), we have

$$
I _ {1} = \widetilde {O} \left(\sqrt {T} \sqrt {d \bar {H} ^ {2} + d ^ {2} \bar {H}} + d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} + \sqrt {d} \bar {H} ^ {1. 5} + d \sqrt {\bar {H} \operatorname {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T)}\right), \tag {D.24}
$$

where we again use the fact  $K(T) = \widetilde{O}(d)$ . Substituting (D.24) into (D.17) and rearranging it, we have

$\operatorname{Regret}'(T)$  
$\leq 4 \gamma \bar{H}^{2} \sqrt{2 T \log(1 / \delta)} + 2 \gamma \bar{H}^{2} K(T) + 2 \bar{H} T \gamma^{U} + \bar{H} I_{1}$  
=  $\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{T}\sqrt{d\bar{H}^4 + d^2\bar{H}^3} +d^{2.5}\bar{H}^2 +\sqrt{d}\bar{H}^{2.5} + \bar{H} T\gamma^U +d\sqrt{\bar{H}^3\mathrm{Regret}'(T)}\right),$

where we use Lemma D.5. Therefore, by the fact  $x = \widetilde{O}(a\sqrt{x} + b) \Rightarrow x = \widetilde{O}(a^2 + b)$ , we have

$$
\mathrm {R e g r e t} ^ {\prime} (T) = \widetilde {O} \bigg (\sqrt {T} \sqrt {d \bar {H} ^ {4} + d ^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {3}} + d ^ {2. 5} \bar {H} ^ {2} + d ^ {2} \bar {H} ^ {3} + \bar {H} T \gamma^ {U} \bigg).
$$

Finally, by Lemma D.3 we have  $\mathrm{Regret}(T) \leq \mathrm{Regret}'(T)$ , finishing our proof.
